id: "12001341"
title: "Armenian genocide recognition"
---

# Armenian genocide recognition



The [eternal flame](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eternal_flame) at the center of the twelve slabs, located at the [Armenian Genocide Memorial complex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tsitsernakaberd) in [Yerevan](34352.md), Armenia

The [recognition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genocide_recognition_politics) of the [Armenian genocide](140376.md) is the acknowledgement of the fact that the [Ottoman Empire](22278.md)'s systematic massacres and forced deportation of [Armenians](387816.md) from 1915 to 1923, both during and after the [First World War](4764461.md), constituted [genocide](12441.md).

Outside of [Turkey](11125639.md) and [Azerbaijan](746.md), the historical consensus is that the Ottoman Empire's persecution of Armenians was a genocide.[^cite_note-academic_consensus-1][^cite_note-2][^cite_note-3] Despite this consensus, some governments have been reticent to officially acknowledge the killings as genocide, due to political concerns regarding their relations with the Turkish government.[^cite_note-4]

The Turkish government has demanded [Armenia](10918072.md) abandon genocide recognition efforts as a condition for normalizing diplomatic relations and lifting the ongoing [Turkish-Azeri blockade of Armenia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish%E2%80%93Azeri_blockade_of_Armenia).[^cite_note-5][^cite_note-6] In contrast, Armenia has consistently advocated for establishing diplomatic relations with Turkey and reopening the border without any preconditions.[^cite_note-7][^cite_note-8][^cite_note-9]

As of 2026, the governments and parliaments of 34 countries, including [Argentina](18951905.md), [Austria](26964606.md), [Brazil](3383.md), [Canada](5042916.md), [France](5843419.md), [Germany](11867.md), [Greece](12108.md), [Italy](14532.md), [Mexico](3966054.md), the [Netherlands](21148.md), [Poland](22936.md), [Portugal](23033.md), [Russia](25391.md), [Sweden](5058739.md), the [United States](3434750.md) and [Uruguay](31843.md), have formally recognized the Armenian genocide, Uruguay having been the first nation to do so.[^cite_note-10] The governments of Azerbaijan, Turkey, and [Pakistan](23235.md) deny the genocide.

## Opinion polls

In 2015, the [Foundation for the Memory of the Shoah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foundation_for_the_Memory_of_the_Shoah) and [Fondapol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fondapol) surveyed 31,172 people between the ages of 16 and 29 living in 31 countries, asking, "In your view, can we talk about genocide in relation to the massacre of the Armenians, by the Turks, in 1915?" 77% of respondents answered in the affirmative. The highest percentage was for France with 93% agreeing and the lowest percentage was for Turkey, with 33% agreeing.[^cite_note-fondapol2015-11][^cite_note-12]

## International organizations

### United Nations

#### The 1948 UN War Crimes Commission Report

On May 15, 1948, the [United Nations Economic and Social Council](31958.md) presented a 384-page report prepared by the [United Nations War Crimes Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_War_Crimes_Commission) (UNWCC),[^cite_note-W\.19-13] set up in London (October 1943) to collect and collate information on war crimes and war criminals.[^cite_note-Avedian2018-14]<sup>: 129 </sup> The report, presented by Lebanese representative [Karim Azkoul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karim_Azkoul),[^cite_note-15] was a response to the request by the UN Secretary-General to make arrangements for "the collection and publication of information concerning human rights arising from trials of war criminals, quislings and traitors, and in particular from the [Nuremberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuremberg_trials) and [Tokyo Trials](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Military_Tribunal_for_the_Far_East)." The report had been prepared by members of the legal staff of the commission. The report is highly topical in regard to the Armenian genocide, not only because it uses the 1915 events as a historic example, but also as a precedent to Articles 6 (c) and 5 (c) of the [Nuremberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuremberg_Charter) and [Tokyo Charters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tokyo_Charter), and thereby as a precursor to the then newly adopted UN [Genocide Convention](143988.md), differentiating between war crimes and crimes against humanity. By referring to the information collected during WWI and put forward by the 1919 [Commission of Responsibilities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commission_of_Responsibilities), the report entitled "Information Concerning Human Rights Arising from Trials of War Criminals" used the Armenian case as a vivid example of crimes committed by a state against its own citizens.

The report also noted that while the [Paris Peace Treaties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paris_Peace_Conference,_1919) with Germany, Austria, Hungary and Bulgaria, did not include any reference to "laws of humanity", instead basing the charges on violations of "[laws and customs of war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Law_of_war)", the [Sèvres Peace Treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_S%C3%A8vres) with Turkey did so. In addition according to Armenian-Swedish historian Avedian Vahagn, Articles 226-228 also did, concerning customs of war (corresponding to Articles 228-230 of the [Treaty of Versailles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Versailles)), the Sèvres Treaty also contained an additional Article 230, obviously in compliance with the Allied ultimatum of May 24, 1915, in regard to "crimes \[committed] against humanity and civilization".[^cite_note-Avedian2018-14]<sup>: 130 </sup>

#### The 1985 UN Genocide Report, the "Whitaker Report"

In 1985, the [United Nations](31769.md) [Sub-Commission on Prevention of Discrimination and Protection of Minorities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sub-Commission_on_Prevention_of_Discrimination_and_Protection_of_Minorities) received a report from Special Rapporteur and Sub-Commission member [Benjamin Whitaker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ben_Whitaker_\(politician\)) (United Kingdom) entitled *Revised and Updated Report on the Question of the Prevention and Punishment of the Crime of Genocide* (commonly known as [The Whitaker Report](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Whitaker_Report_\(United_Nations\))), in which the systematic Ottoman massacre of Armenians during World War I was cited as meeting the criteria for the UN definition of genocide and as one of the genocides of the 20th century.[^cite_note-unicj-16] His report was received and noted by a resolution at the 38th session of the Sub-Commission in 1985.[^cite_note-17]

### Catholic Church

In 2015, [Pope Francis](1687680.md) said that the Armenian genocide was "considered the first genocide of the 20th century." The word was not used again, due to strong Turkish backlash, until June 2016, when he reaffirmed and solidified his stance on it constituting a genocide and strongly condemned the enduring denial of the genocide.[^cite_note-18] Turkey responded by accusing the Pope of having a "[crusader](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crusade) mentality" against the country. The Vatican strongly denied this, claiming that the Pope had actually called for reconciliation between Armenians and Turks.[^cite_note-19]

### International Association of Genocide Scholars

In 1997, the [International Association of Genocide Scholars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Association_of_Genocide_Scholars) (IAGS) passed a resolution unanimously recognizing the Ottoman massacres of Armenians as genocide.[^cite_note-20][^cite_note-21]

### International Center for Transitional Justice

In February 2002, an independent legal opinion commissioned by [Turkish Armenian Reconciliation Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_Armenian_Reconciliation_Commission) and published by the [International Center for Transitional Justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Center_for_Transitional_Justice) concluded that the Ottoman massacre of Armenians in 1915–1918 were a genocide, but that no legal consequences flowed from this fact because genocide was only criminalized in international law after the ratification of the [Genocide Convention](143988.md).[^cite_note-22]

In 2007, the [Elie Wiesel Foundation for Humanity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elie_Wiesel_Foundation_for_Humanity) wrote a letter[^cite_note-23] signed by 53 [Nobel laureates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nobel_laureate) re-affirming the Genocide Scholars' conclusion that the 1915 killings of Armenians constituted genocide.[^cite_note-24] [Wiesel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elie_Wiesel)'s organization also asserted that Turkish acknowledgement of the Armenian genocide would create no legal "basis for reparations or territorial claims", anticipating Turkish anxieties that it could prompt financial or territorial claims.[^cite_note-25]

### European Parliament

On April 15, 2015, the [European Parliament](9581.md) backed a motion that calls the massacre of up to 1.5 million Armenians by Ottoman Turkish forces a genocide and urged for a restoration of diplomatic relations,[^cite_note-26] days after Pope Francis's message triggered an angry reaction in Turkey for using the same term.[^cite_note-27] It had previously done so in 1987,[^cite_note-28] 2000,[^cite_note-29] 2002[^cite_note-30] and 2005.[^cite_note-31]

The European Parliament commended the message the pontiff had delivered.[^cite_note-europarl-32][^cite_note-reut-33][^cite_note-nytimes-34] Prior to the vote, [Turkish president](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_Turkey) [Erdoğan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recep_Tayyip_Erdo%C4%9Fan) declared that "Turkey will ignore any decision by the European parliament", and that "I personally don't bother about a defence because \[Turks] don't carry a stain or a shadow like genocide".[^cite_note-35]

### Council of Europe

The [Council of Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_Europe) passed a resolution on the Armenian genocide on May 14, 2001, which commits only the members who signed it, and appeals "to all the members of the Parliamentary Assembly of the Council of Europe to take the necessary steps for the recognition of the genocide perpetrated by the Ottoman Empire against the Armenians at the beginning of the 20th century."[^cite_note-36]

### American Jewish organizations

On November 7, 1989, the [Union for Reform Judaism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_for_Reform_Judaism) passed a resolution on recognition of the Armenian genocide.[^cite_note-37]

In 2007 the [Anti-Defamation League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Defamation_League) declared the following press statement:

> We have never negated but have always described the painful events of 1915–1918 perpetrated by the Ottoman Empire against the Armenians as massacres and atrocities. On reflection, we have come to share the view of [Henry Morgenthau, Sr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry_Morgenthau_Sr.) that the consequences of those actions were indeed tantamount to genocide. If the word genocide had existed then, they would have called it genocide.[^cite_note-38]

In 2014 the [American Jewish Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Jewish_Committee) recognized the Armenian genocide as a historical fact.[^cite_note-ajc-39][^cite_note-asb-40]

In October 2015, the [Jewish Council for Public Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_Council_for_Public_Affairs) published a resolution calling on the U.S. government to recognize the World War I-era Turkish massacres of Armenians as a genocide.[^cite_note-41]

### Central Council of Jews in Germany

In April 2015, the [Central Council of Jews in Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Council_of_Jews_in_Germany) called on the German government to recognize the World War I mass murder of over one million Armenians in what was then the Ottoman Empire as a genocide. "One hundred years ago, the government of the Ottoman Empire ordered the deportation of one million Armenians. They were murdered directly, or died of starvation and dehydration in the desert," Central Council President [Josef Schuster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Josef_Schuster) told the newspaper *[Der Tagesspiegel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Der_Tagesspiegel)*. He added: "These terrible events should be called what they were: a genocide." Schuster said the Armenian genocide later served [Adolf Hitler](2731583.md) and his Nazis as a blueprint for the Holocaust.[^cite_note-wjc-42][^cite_note-43][^cite_note-44]

## Parliaments and governments

Turkey continues to insist that the mass killings of 1915 were not a genocide, a fact which many [Europeans](9239.md) take as casting doubt on the Turkish nation's commitment to human rights, causing them to oppose [European Union](9317.md) membership a stance for which [Turkish-Armenian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenians_in_Turkey) intellectual [Hrant Dink](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hrant_Dink) publicly issued condemnation to some countries before he was [assassinated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assassination_of_Hrant_Dink) in 2007.[^cite_note-59][^cite_note-60]

### Countries



&#x20; Countries that have recognized the genocide by a resolution in their national legislature

&#x20; Countries that explicitly deny that there was an Armenian genocide

On May 24, 1915, during World War I, the [Allied Powers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allies_of_World_War_I) (the United Kingdom, France and Russia) jointly issued a statement in which they said that for approximately a month, the Kurdish and Turkish populations of Armenia had been massacring Armenians, with the connivance and often assistance of Ottoman authorities, and that the Allied Powers would hold all officers of the Ottoman Government implicated in such crimes personally responsible for [crimes against humanity](61099.md).[^cite_note-CAH1915-61][^cite_note-62][^cite_note-63]

On April 20, 1965, [Uruguay](31843.md) became the first nation in the world to officially recognize the Armenian genocide.[^cite_note-64] During the second half of the 20th century and the 21st century, parliaments of several countries have formally recognized the event as genocide. [Turkish entry talks with the European Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Accession_of_Turkey_to_the_European_Union) were met with a number of calls to consider the event as genocide,[^cite_note-65][^cite_note-66][^cite_note-67] though it never became a precondition.

As of June 2026, 36 states had officially recognized the historical events as genocide. Sovereign nations (i.e. [UN member-states](31969.md)) officially recognizing the Armenian genocide are:[^cite_note-68][^cite_note-69]

| Country | Year(s) of recognition | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [Argentina](18951905.md) | 1993, 2003, 2004, 2005, 2006, 2007[^cite_note-Genocide-70][^cite_note-71] |  |
| [Armenia](10918072.md) | 1988[^cite_note-72] | Recognition extended by the [Armenian SSR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_SSR). |
| [Austria](26964606.md) | 2015[^cite_note-\:4-73][^cite_note-74] |  |
| [Belgium](3343.md) | 1998,[^cite_note-75] 2015[^cite_note-76] |  |
| [Bolivia](3462.md) | 2014 | The resolution was approved unanimously by both the [Senate and the Chamber of Deputies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plurinational_Legislative_Assembly), with the approval of the [Foreign Ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Bolivia\)).[^cite_note-77] |
| [Brazil](3383.md) | 2015 | The resolution was approved by the [Federal Senate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_Senate).[^cite_note-78][^cite_note-79] |
| [Bulgaria](3415.md) | 2015 | The declaration was adopted by the [Bulgarian parliament](2122384.md) on April 24, 2015, using the phrase "mass extermination of the Armenian People in the Ottoman Empire" and declares April 24 as a Victims Remembrance Day.[^cite_note-standartnews.com-80][^cite_note-81][^cite_note-bta.bg-82] Regarding the usage of "mass extermination" the Bulgarian Prime Minister [Boyko Borisov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boyko_Borisov) has specified the following: "I said it very clearly: this is the Bulgarian word or the Bulgarian words, or the Bulgarian idiom for 'genocide.'" [^cite_note-bta.bg-82] |
| [Canada](5042916.md) | 1996, 2002, 2004,[^cite_note-83] 2006[^cite_note-84] |  |
| [Chile](5489.md) | 2007, 2015[^cite_note-85][^cite_note-86] |  |
| [Cyprus](5593.md) | 1975, 1982, 1990 | The first country to raise the issue to the [United Nations General Assembly](31957.md). Denial of the genocide is criminalized in Cyprus.[^cite_note-87] |
| [Czechia](5321.md) | 2017, 2020[^cite_note-88] | On April 14, 2015, Czech Committee on Foreign Affairs of the [Chamber of Deputies](3991120.md) passed a resolution on the occasion of the Armenian Genocide Centenary.[^cite_note-89] On April 25, 2017 [Czech Parliament](3991200.md) approved a resolution condemning the Armenian genocide.[^cite_note-90] On May 20, 2020, the [Czech Senate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Czech_Senate) unanimously adopted a resolution recognizing the Armenian genocide.[^cite_note-91] |
| [France](5843419.md) | 1998, 2001[^cite_note-auron-92] | On February 5, 2019, French president [Emmanuel Macron](43671127.md) declared April 24 as Armenian genocide commemoration day in [France](5843419.md).[^cite_note-News.am-93] |
| [Germany](11867.md) | 2005, 2016[^cite_note-94] | Resolution passed first reading in April 2015. On June 2, 2016, the [German Bundestag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_Bundestag) almost unanimously (with one vote against and one abstention) passed a resolution qualifying the Ottoman-era Armenian killings 'genocide'.[^cite_note-95] |
| [Greece](12108.md) | 1996 | Denial of the genocide is criminalized. Punishable by up to 3 years in prison and a fine not to exceed €30,000, per 2014 act.[^cite_note-96] |
| [Italy](14532.md) | 2000, 2019[^cite_note-auron-92][^cite_note-guardian-97] | Denial of genocides is criminalized. It stipulates 3-year imprisonment and a fine.[^cite_note-98][^cite_note-99][^cite_note-100] On April 10, 2019, the [Italian Chamber of Deputies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Chamber_of_Deputies) adopted an initiative calling on the Italian government to recognize the Armenian genocide and give the issue an international dimension.[^cite_note-https-101] |
| [Latvia](17514.md) | 2021 | On May 6, 2021, the [Latvian Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latvian_Parliament), or Saeima, approved a resolution recognizing the Armenian genocide by 58 to 11 votes with 7 abstentions.[^cite_note-Latvia_recognizes-102][^cite_note-103] |
| [Libya](17633.md) | 2019 | The Eastern-based interim administration's Council of Ministers has adopted a proposal from Abdelhady Alhweij, its foreign minister, to recognize the Armenian genocide. Libya is the third Arab (the other two being Lebanon and Syria) and the first African state to recognize the genocide.[^cite_note-104] According to the [Armenian ministry of Foreign Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Armenia\)), Libya currently doesn't recognize the genocide.[^cite_note-105] |
| [Lithuania](17675.md) | 2005[^cite_note-106] | On December 16, 2005, the Lithuanian Parliament, or Seimas, approved a resolution recognizing the Armenian genocide. |
| [Lebanon](17771.md) | 1997, 2000[^cite_note-107] | On May 11, 2000, the Lebanese Parliament unanimously approved a resolution calling for the commemoration of the 85th anniversary of the Armenian genocide perpetrated by the Ottoman Turkish government at the beginning of the century. The resolution, presented by the parliament executive, calls on all Lebanese citizens to unite with the Armenian people on April 24 in commemorating the atrocities of 1915–1923. |
| [Luxembourg](17515.md) | 2015[^cite_note-108] | The [Chamber of Deputies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chamber_of_Deputies_\(Luxembourg\)) unanimously adopted a resolution on the recognition of the genocide of Armenian people.[^cite_note-109] |
| [Mexico](3966054.md) | 2023[^cite_note-110] | On February 8, 2023, the [Mexican Senate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican_Senate) adopted a document on Armenian Genocide recognition citing the need to protect universal human rights.[^cite_note-111] |
| [Netherlands](21148.md) | 2004, 2015,[^cite_note-112][^cite_note-113] 2018[^cite_note-114][^cite_note-115] | The Dutch government will send a Minister or State Secretary to Armenia to attend [Armenian Genocide Remembrance Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_Genocide_Remembrance_Day) every five years, starting in 2018.[^cite_note-116] |
| [Paraguay](23420.md) | 2015 | The [Chamber of Senators](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senate_of_Paraguay) in Paraguay unanimously adopted the resolution.[^cite_note-117] |
| [Poland](22936.md) | 2005[^cite_note-asbarezP-118] | The *[Sejm](28167.md)* of the Republic of Poland (lower house of the Polish parliament) unanimously passed a bill recognizing the Armenian genocide on April 19, 2005. |
| [Portugal](23033.md) | 2019[^cite_note-119] | On April 26, 2019, the Parliament of Portugal voted for the recognition of the Armenian genocide perpetrated by the Ottoman Empire, with a Vote of Regret N.º 819/XIII. |
| [Russia](25391.md) | 1995, 2005, 2015[^cite_note-naryshkin-120][^cite_note-armenianow-121] |  |
| [Slovakia](26830.md) | 2004[^cite_note-122] | Denial of the genocide is criminalized. Punishable by up to 5 years in prison, per 2011 act.[^cite_note-123] |
| [Sweden](5058739.md) | 2010[^cite_note-124] | Swedish parliament passed a resolution recognizing the Armenian genocide as well as the [Assyrian genocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assyrian_genocide) and [Greek genocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greek_genocide), but the foreign minister said that this resolution did not reflect the position of the executive branch. Turkey recalled its ambassador from Stockholm.[^cite_note-125] |
| [Switzerland](26748.md) | 2003[^cite_note-126] | Denial of the genocide is criminalized. See also: [Perinçek v. Switzerland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Perin%C3%A7ek_v._Switzerland) |
| [Syria](7515849.md) | 2015, 2020[^cite_note-127] | On February 13, 2020, the [People's Assembly of Syria](4504009.md) unanimously adopted a resolution recognizing and condemning the Armenian genocide.[^cite_note-128] |
| [United States](3434750.md) | 2019, 2021[^cite_note-129][^cite_note-USSenate-130][^cite_note-131] | The [United States Congress](31756.md) officially recognizes the Armenian genocide. The [House of Representatives](19468510.md) affirmed the United States record on the Armenian genocide with House Resolution 296 on October 29, 2019.[^cite_note-132] The [Senate](24909346.md) unanimously recognized the genocide with Senate Resolution 150 on December 12, 2019.[^cite_note-USSenate-130][^cite_note-133] However, President [Donald Trump](4848272.md) did not support the resolution, citing that his administration's stance on the issue had not changed.[^cite_note-134] On April 24, 2021, President [Joe Biden](145422.md) officially recognized the Armenian genocide.[^cite_note-135] |
| [Uruguay](31843.md) | 1965, 2004, 2015[^cite_note-136][^cite_note-137] | The first country to recognize the events as genocide. On May 14, 2024, the [Chamber of Representatives of Uruguay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chamber_of_Representatives_of_Uruguay) unanimously approved a bill that declares April 24 of each year as "Armenian Genocide Remembrance Day".[^cite_note-138] |
| [Holy See](13393.md) | 2000, 2015[^cite_note-139] |  |
| [Venezuela](32374.md) | 2005[^cite_note-140] | On July 14, 2005, the [National Assembly of Venezuela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Assembly_\(Venezuela\)) adopted a resolution that recognizes the Armenian genocide. |

### States, regions, provinces, municipalities and parliamentary committees

Recognition of the Armenian genocide also includes:

Australia

Parliaments of 3 Australian states recognize the Armenian genocide:

-  [New South Wales](21654.md): In 2007 the Parliament of the State of New South Wales passed a motion condemning the genocide and called on the Australian Federal Government to do the same.

  [^cite_note-141]

-  [South Australia](26716.md): In March 2009 the Parliament of South Australia passed a similar motion to that passed in New South Wales in 2007.

  [^cite_note-142]

-  [Tasmania](29944.md): On May 11, 2023, the House Assembly of the State of Tasmania unanimously passed a motion recognizing and condemning the Armenian, Greek, and Assyrian Genocides of 1915.

  [^cite_note-143]

Additionally, the [City of Ryde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_of_Ryde) adopted a unanimous motion dedicated to the centenary of the Armenian genocide at its Council Meeting on April 14, 2015. The motion further calls upon the Government of Australia to recognize and condemn all genocides.[^cite_note-144] The [City of Willoughby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_of_Willoughby) passed a motion recognizing the Armenian genocide on May 11, 2015.[^cite_note-145] The [City of Parramatta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_of_Parramatta) unanimously adopted a motion on recognition of the Armenian genocide on April 21, 2025.[^cite_note-146]

Belgium

-  [Flanders](10878.md): The Parliament of Flanders adopted a resolution recognizing the Armenian genocide and calling upon Turkey to come to terms with its past.

  [^cite_note-147]

Brazil

The parliaments of 4 Brazilian states recognize the Armenian genocide:

-  [Ceará](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cear%C3%A1): In 2006, The Legislative Council of State of Ceará, Brazil, unanimously passed a bill which recognizes April 24 as the Day of the Armenian People. Armenian genocide victims will henceforth be commemorated on this day.

  [^cite_note-148]

-  [Paraná](229411.md): In 2013, the parliament of the state of Paraná adopted a bill recognizing the Armenian genocide in the Ottoman Empire.

  [^cite_note-149]

-  [Rio de Janeiro](229419.md): The state of Rio de Janeiro recognized the Armenian genocide on July 24, 2015, with a law setting April 24 as "Day of recognition and memory of the victims of the Armenian Genocide".

  [^cite_note-150]

-  [São Paulo](229422.md): The [Legislative Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legislative_Assembly_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo) of the largest Brazilian state – São Paulo – passed a law recognizing April 24 as "a Commemoration Day of the 1.5 million victims of the Armenian Genocide in 1915" in 2003.

  [^cite_note-151]

Bulgaria

-  [Bulgaria](3415.md): 7 provinces (*oblasts*) have recognized the Armenian genocide – first in 2008 was the [Plovdiv Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plovdiv_Province) followed by the provinces of [Burgas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burgas_Province), [Ruse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruse_Province), [Stara Zagora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stara_Zagora_Province), [Pazardzhik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pazardzhik_Province), [Haskovo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haskovo_Municipality) and [Svilengrad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Svilengrad_Municipality).

Canada

-  [Alberta](717.md): The [Legislative Assembly of Alberta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legislative_Assembly_of_Alberta) unanimously passed a law which recognizes the Armenian genocide and [other genocides](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genocides_in_history) in 2021.

  [^cite_note-152]

-  [British Columbia](3392.md): The [Legislative Assembly of British Columbia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legislative_Assembly_of_British_Columbia) recognized the Armenian genocide.

  [^cite_note-153]

-  [Ontario](22218.md): The [Legislative Assembly of Ontario](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legislative_Assembly_of_Ontario) unanimously adopted a resolution recognizing the Armenian genocide in 1980.

  [^cite_note-154]

-  [Quebec](7954867.md): Quebec was the first province in Canada to recognize the Armenian genocide in 1980.

  [^cite_note-155]

Additionally, the cities of [Montreal](7954681.md) and [Toronto](64646.md) recognized the Armenian genocide.[^cite_note-156]

-  [Egypt](8087628.md): In February 2019, the President of Egypt [Abdel Fattah el-Sisi](36703624.md) addressed his recognition of the Armenian genocide during the [2019 Munich Security Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/55th_Munich_Security_Conference)

[Armenian Genocide Remembrance Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_Genocide_Remembrance_Day) in [Saint Sarkis Cathedral, Tehran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Sarkis_Cathedral,_Tehran), 2017 (center: Archbishop [Sepuh Sargsyan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sepuh_Sargsyan))

Israel

-  [Israel](9282173.md): On August 1, 2016, the [Knesset](74656.md)'s [Education, Culture and Sports Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Knesset_Committee_on_Education,_Culture_and_Sports) announced its recognition of the Armenian genocide and urged the Israeli government to formally acknowledge the 1915 mass slaughter of 1.5 million Armenians as such.

  [^cite_note-161]

  &#x20;On March 30, 2023, the City Council of [Haifa](55606.md) recognized the genocide and became the second city in Israel to do so, after [Petah Tikva](202411.md).

  [^cite_note-162]

  &#x20;On June 28, 2026, the entire Israeli government recognised the Armenian genocide, marking a break from the country's previous politics.

## Positions by countries

### Turkey

See also: [Armenian genocide denial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_genocide_denial)



Armenian genocide centennial commemoration near [Taksim Square](631961.md), Istanbul in 2015.

The verdict of the [Istanbul trials](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_courts-martial_of_1919%E2%80%9320), held by the Ottoman government in 1919–1920, acknowledged the massacre of Armenians as "war crimes", and sentenced the perpetrators to death.[^cite_note-antos-244] However, in 1921, during the resurgence of the [Turkish National Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_National_Movement), amnesty was given to those found guilty. According to Jason D. Antos, "every other Turkish government" denied the genocide.[^cite_note-antos-244][^cite_note-huberta-245][^cite_note-246][^cite_note-247]

A major obstacle for wider recognition of the genocide in the world is the official position of Turkey, which rests on the assumption that the deportation of Armenians was a [legitimate state action](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genocide_justification).[^cite_note-248] According to the Turkish government, the events of 1915 were a "tragedy" that resulted in "the loss of many innocent lives" but they cannot be described as a genocide.[^cite_note-249][^cite_note-250]

In April 2006, the Turkish [Human Rights Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_Rights_Association_\(Turkey\)) (IHD) recognized the events as a genocide.[^cite_note-251]

In December 2008, a group of Turkish intellectuals launched an [online petition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Online_petition) – named the "[I Apologize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I_Apologize_campaign)" campaign ('Özür Diliyorum' in Turkish) – for people wanting to apologize in a personal capacity. The writers of the petition used the word "the Great Catastrophe" regarding the events. The petition gained upwards of 10,000 signatures in a matter of days. In the face of a backlash,[^cite_note-252] then Turkish president [Abdullah Gül](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdullah_G%C3%BCl) defended the petition, citing freedom of speech.[^cite_note-253] An opposition group soon launched a website raising an even higher number of signatures. [Recep Tayyip Erdogan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recep_Tayyip_Erdogan), at the time [prime minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Turkey), denied there was anything to apologize for, but also didn't oppose the campaign and a national debate ensued.[^cite_note-254][^cite_note-255]

Since the "I Apologize" campaign in 2008, every year on April 24, commemoration ceremonies for the genocide are held in several Turkish cities. They started at [Taksim Square](631961.md) in [Istanbul](3391396.md) in 2008, mainly as a result of the nationwide discussion that came after the [assassination of Hrant Dink](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assassination_of_Hrant_Dink) and then spread to [Ankara](802.md), [Diyarbakır](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diyarbak%C4%B1r), [İzmir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C4%B0zmir), [Malatya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malatya), and [Mersin](1146241.md) in the following years.[^cite_note-256] The commemorations draw increasing support each year.

Below are the questions asked and the percentages of the answers given in a 2014 poll for [The Centre for Economics and Foreign Policy Studies (EDAM)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Centre_for_Economics_and_Foreign_Policy_Studies_\(EDAM\)?action=edit\&redlink=1), a Turkish think-tank:[^cite_note-EDAM-257][^cite_note-EDAM-archived-258]

| Question | % of answers | % of answers (excluding "No idea/No response") | % of answers among Turkish foreign policy experts |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Turkey should apologize for the Armenians that lost their lives in 1915 and admit that what happened was a genocide | 9.1% | 12.1% | 18.7% |
| Turkey should apologize for the Armenians that lost their lives in 1915 but should take no other steps | 9.1% | 12.1% | 26.0% |
| Turkey should express its regret over the Armenians that lost their lives in 1915 but should not apologize | 12.0% | 16% | 17.3% |
| Turkey should express that not everyone that lost their lives in 1915 were Armenians and express its regret for all the Ottoman citizens that perished in that period | 23.5% | 31.3% | 36.7% |
| Turkey should take no steps | 21.3% | 28.4% | 1.3% |
| No idea/No response | 25.0% | - | - |
| *Total* | *100%* | *100%* | *100%* |
| *Total "Turkey should apologize for the Armenians that lost their lives in 1915"* | *18.2%* | *24.3%* | *44.7%* |
| *Total "Turkey should apologize" + "express its regret over the Armenians that lost their lives in 1915"* | *30.2%* | *34.2%* | *62.0%* |

Answers to the poll varied greatly depending on the political party, from 4.6% of [MHP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nationalist_Movement_Party) voters who recognize the Armenian genocide to 24.4% of [BDP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peace_and_Democracy_Party) voters. Excluding "No idea/No response", 51% of [CHP](404748.md) voters and 44% of BDP voters answered that "Turkey should apologize for the Armenians that lost their lives" or "Turkey should express its regret over the Armenians that lost their lives in 1915 but should not apologize".[^cite_note-EDAM-archived-258]

&#x20;Since 2014, President Erdoğan has sent a message every year on April 24—the [Armenian Genocide Remembrance Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_Genocide_Remembrance_Day)—to the [Armenian Patriarch of Constantinople](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_Patriarch_of_Constantinople), in Turkish and Armenian, to offer his condolences to Armenians for the "events of 1915", without using the word "genocide". It is the first time a Turkish leader has formally offered condolences for the mass killings.[^cite_note-259][^cite_note-260] In 2021, his message included:&#x20;

> I remember with respect the Ottoman Armenians, who lost their lives under the harsh conditions of the First World War, and offer my condolences to their grandchildren. \[...] I thank our Armenian citizens, who are an inseparable part of our nation, for their sincere support to this struggle our country has been putting up. With these thoughts in my mind, I once again remember with respect the Ottoman Armenians whom we lost during the First World War, share the grief of their relatives and offer my regards and affection to you all.[^cite_note-261][^cite_note-262]

In a 2015 poll for the [Foundation for the Memory of Shoah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foundation_for_the_Memory_of_Shoah?action=edit\&redlink=1) and [Fondapol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fondapol), 33% of people between the ages of 16 and 29 living in Turkey surveyed answered in the affirmative to the question: "In your view, can we talk about genocide in relation to the massacre of the Armenians, by the Turks, in 1915?".[^cite_note-fondapol2015-11]

In the twentieth century, the only Turkish political movement to recognize the genocide was the [Maoist](46636.md) militant group [Communist Party of Turkey/Marxist–Leninist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist_Party_of_Turkey/Marxist%E2%80%93Leninist) (TKP/ML).[^cite_note-263] The genocide has also been recognized by Kurdish political movements including the [Kurdistan Workers' Party](69680.md) (PKK) in its official newspaper in 1982 and the [Kurdish parliament-in-exile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurdish_parliament-in-exile?action=edit\&redlink=1) in 1997.[^cite_note-264] As of 2020, genocide denial is supported by all major political parties in Turkey, except the [Peoples' Democratic Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peoples'_Democratic_Party_\(Turkey\)) (HDP) and the [Green Left Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_Left_Party), as well as many pro- and anti-government media and civil society organizations. Both government and opposition parties have strongly reacted to genocide recognition in other countries.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGalip202087,_163-265][^cite_note-266] The socialist [Workers' Party of Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Workers'_Party_of_Turkey_\(2017\)) has expressed opposition against the actions, while not expressly calling it a genocide.[^cite_note-267] On July 18, 2023, the [European Parliament](9581.md) called on Turkey to recognize the Armenian genocide.[^cite_note-268]

### Kurdish position

Further information: [Kurdish recognition of the Armenian genocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurdish_recognition_of_the_Armenian_genocide)

Some Kurdish tribes played a role in the genocide, as they were utilized by the Ottoman authorities to carry out the mass killings.[^cite_note-269] Among modern Kurds, including major Kurdish parties like the [Peoples' Democratic Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peoples'_Democratic_Party_\(Turkey\)) (HDP), and the [Kurdistan Democratic Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurdistan_Democratic_Party) (KDP), most of them acknowledge the killings and apologize in the name of their ancestors who committed atrocities toward Armenians, [Greeks](42056.md), and [Assyrians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assyrian_people) in the name of the Ottoman Empire.[^cite_note-270][^cite_note-271][^cite_note-272][^cite_note-hurriyet.com.tr-273][^cite_note-274]

According to a 2014 poll, 24.4% of Turkish citizens who vote for [BDP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peace_and_Democracy_Party) ([HDP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peoples'_Democratic_Party_\(Turkey\))), which is usually voted by Kurds, accept the Armenian genocide. Excluding those who didn't answer the question in the poll, 44% of BDP voters think that "Turkey should apologize for the Armenians that lost their lives in 1915" or that "Turkey should express its regret over the Armenians that lost their lives in 1915".[^cite_note-EDAM-archived-258]

### United States

Further information: [United States recognition of the Armenian genocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_recognition_of_the_Armenian_genocide)



[Armenian Genocide Remembrance Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_Genocide_Remembrance_Day) in [Los Angeles](18110.md), [California](5407.md), 2015

In 1951, the [United States Department of State](31975.md) exemplified the Armenian case as an act of genocide in recent past, in the written statement by United States to the [International Court of Justice](14918.md) (ICJ): "The Genocide Convention resulted from the inhuman and barbarous practices which prevailed in certain countries prior to and during World War II, when entire religious, racial and national minority groups were threatened with and subjected to deliberate extermination. The practice of genocide has occurred throughout human history. The [Roman persecution of the Christians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persecution_of_Christians_in_the_Roman_Empire), the Turkish massacres of Armenians, the extermination of millions of Jews and Poles by the Nazis are outstanding examples of the crime of genocide.

This was the background when the General Assembly of the United Nations considered the problem of genocide."[^cite_note-US-ICJ1951-275] That the US later changed this position and has since consistently refrained from officially using the term "genocide" about these events can be ascribed to the rise of the Cold War, Turkey's NATO membership and the disappearance of [Raphael Lemkin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raphael_Lemkin) as a strong human rights advocate from the US State Department.[^cite_note-Avedian2018-14]

Several official US documents describe the events as genocide. The [United States House of Representatives](19468510.md) adopted resolutions commemorating the Armenian genocide in 1975,[^cite_note-Portraits_of_hope-276][^cite_note-gov-277] 1984[^cite_note-278] and 1996.[^cite_note-279] President [Ronald Reagan](25433.md) also described the events as genocide in his speech on April 22, 1981.[^cite_note-280] On March 4, 2010, the [United States House Committee on Foreign Affairs](1267657.md) recognized the massacres of 1915 as genocide.[^cite_note-281] The legislatures of all 50 US states have made individual proclamations recognizing the events of 1915 to 1923 as genocide, with [Alabama](303.md) in 2019 and [Mississippi](16949861.md) in 2022 being the last.[^cite_note-usast-282][^cite_note-The_Armenians_of_Cyprus-283]

House Joint Resolution 148, adopted on April 8, 1975, resolved:

> Authorizes the President to designate April 24, 1975, as 'National Day of Remembrance of Man's Inhumanity to Man' for remembrance of all the victims of genocide, especially those of Armenian ancestry who succumbed to the genocide perpetrated in 1915.[^cite_note-gov-277]

After the tenure of Ronald Reagan, later United States Presidents, until Joe Biden, refused to name the events as such because of concerns over alienating Turkey.[^cite_note-auto-284][^cite_note-Liptak-285]

The [Armenian Assembly of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_Assembly_of_America) (AAA) and the [Armenian National Committee of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_National_Committee_of_America) (ANCA), advocacy organizations representing the views and values of the Armenian American community in the United States, have been urging Congress and the President of the United States to recognize the genocide by Ottoman Turkey in 1915.[^cite_note-Cameron,_Fraser_pp.91-286] They have also asked for an increase of economic aid to Armenia.

The US House Committee on Foreign Affairs approved [HR 106](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_resolution_on_Armenian_Genocide), a bill that categorized and condemned the Ottoman Empire for the Genocide, on October 10, 2007, by a 27–21 vote. However, some of the support for the bill from both Democrats and Republicans eroded after the [White House](33057.md) warned against the possibility of Turkey restricting [airspace](353557.md) as well as ground-route access for US military and humanitarian efforts in [Iraq](7515928.md) in response to the bill.[^cite_note-auto-284] In response to the House Foreign Affairs Committee's decision on the bill, Turkey ordered their ambassador to the United States to return to Turkey for "consultations".[^cite_note-foxnews.com-287] The [Turkish lobby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_lobby) worked intensely to block the bill's passage.[^cite_note-foxnews.com-287]

On January 19, 2008, United States Senator [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama) stated that "the Armenian Genocide is not an allegation, a personal opinion, or a point of view, but rather a widely documented fact supported by an overwhelming body of historical evidence. The facts are undeniable." He promised to recognize the genocide if elected president.[^cite_note-288] On April 24, 2009, he stated as president: "I have consistently stated my own view of what occurred in 1915, and my view of that history has not changed. My interest remains the achievement of a full, frank and just acknowledgment of the facts."[^cite_note-289] On April 24 commemoration speeches, Obama referred only to the Armenian synonym Mets Eghern ("Mec Eġeṙn"). In 2010,[^cite_note-290][^cite_note-291][^cite_note-292][^cite_note-293] 2012,[^cite_note-294] 2013,[^cite_note-295] 2014[^cite_note-296] 2015,[^cite_note-297][^cite_note-298] and 2016[^cite_note-299] he did not use the word 'genocide'. In April 2015, Obama sent a Presidential Delegation to Armenia to attend the Centennial Commemoration in [Yerevan](34352.md).[^cite_note-300] [Susan E. Rice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Susan_E._Rice), Obama's [National Security Advisor](44028.md), encouraged the Foreign Minister [Mevlüt Çavuşoğlu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mevl%C3%BCt_%C3%87avu%C5%9Fo%C4%9Flu) of Turkey to take concrete steps to improve relations with Armenia and to facilitate an open and frank dialogue in Turkey about the atrocities of 1915.[^cite_note-301]

On October 29, 2019, the [United States House of Representatives](19468510.md) passed a resolution on a 405–11 vote to recognize the Armenian genocide. The [United States Senate](24909346.md) passed the resolution through unanimous vote on December 12, 2019, defying President [Donald Trump](4848272.md), who opposed recognition.[^cite_note-302] On April 24, 2020, President Trump issued a statement on [Armenian Genocide Remembrance Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_Genocide_Remembrance_Day), referring to the events as "one of the worst mass atrocities of the 20th century".[^cite_note-303]

On April 24, 2021, on Armenian Genocide Remembrance Day, President [Joe Biden](145422.md) referred to the events as "genocide" in a statement released by the White House,[^cite_note-304][^cite_note-Liptak-285][^cite_note-Time_2021-305] in which the President formally equated the genocide perpetrated against Armenians with atrocities on the scale of those committed in [Nazi-occupied Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazi-occupied_Europe).[^cite_note-Time_2021-305]

On April 24, 2025, during the first Armenian Genocide Remembrance Day of Trump's [second term](78298108.md), the White House did not use the term "genocide" in a statement commemorating the day.[^cite_note-306] *[The Armenian Weekly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Armenian_Weekly)* referred to Trump's statement as "a shameful reversal of decades of U.S. acknowledgements".[^cite_note-307] [Aram Hamparian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aram_Hamparian), the executive director of the [Armenian National Committee of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_National_Committee_of_America), also condemned the statement, writing "Trump's omission is not a diplomatic oversight – but rather a deliberate retreat from truth and a dangerous signal of U.S. tolerance for ongoing anti-Armenian violence."[^cite_note-308] The second Trump administration continues to avoid using the term "genocide," instead continuing to refer to the events as the *[Medz Yeghern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terminology_of_the_Armenian_genocide#Armenian)* ([Armenian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_language) for 'Great Catastrophe') and labeling April 24 as only "Armenian Remembrance Day."[^cite_note-309]

### France



Armenian Genocide Remembrance Day in [Lyon](8638634.md), 2015

France has formally recognized the Armenian massacres as genocide.[^cite_note-BBC-310]

In 2006, the [French Parliament](1009776.md) submitted a bill to create a law that would punish any person denying the Armenian genocide with up to five years' imprisonment and a fine.[^cite_note-France-311] Despite Turkish protests,[^cite_note-Turkey_warns_France_over_Armenian_Genocide_Bill-312] the [French National Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Assembly_of_France) adopted a bill making it a crime to deny that Armenians suffered genocide in 1915 at the hands of the Ottoman Turks.[^cite_note-313] The bill had been criticized as an attempt to garner votes from among the 600,000 ethnic Armenians of France.[^cite_note-314] This criticism has come not only from within Turkey,[^cite_note-CBCFRENCHBILL-315] but also from [Orhan Pamuk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orhan_Pamuk).[^cite_note-316] However, the bill was dropped in the summer of 2011 before going to the Senate.[^cite_note-317]

Since then, France has urged Turkey to recognize the 1915 massacre as genocide.[^cite_note-318]

The [French Senate](567323.md) passed a bill in 2011 that criminalizes denial of acknowledged genocides, which includes both the Holocaust and the Armenian genocide. The bill was submitted by the parliament in 2012.[^cite_note-319] However, the bill was considered unconstitutional on February 28, 2012, by the [French Constitutional Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_Council_of_France): "The council rules that by punishing anyone contesting the existence of... crimes that lawmakers themselves recognized or qualified as such, lawmakers committed an unconstitutional attack on freedom of expression".[^cite_note-320]

The French Senate adopted a new bill on October 14, 2016, that made the denial of the Armenian genocide a crime. The bill was introduced by the French Government and passed by the French National Assembly in July 2016, and stipulates a penalty of a year in prison or a 45,000 € fine.[^cite_note-france-321] However, the law was put down by the French Constitutional Court in January 2017. The Council said the "ruling causes uncertainty regarding expressions and comments on historical matters. Thereby, this ruling is an unnecessary and disproportionate attack against freedom of speech."[^cite_note-322]

On February 5, 2019, French President Emmanuel Macron declared April 24 as Armenian genocide commemoration day in [France](5843419.md).[^cite_note-News.am-93]

### Iran

Due to the period of weak central government and [Tehran](57654.md)'s inability to protect its territorial integrity when the genocide occurred, Muslim Turks and Kurdish tribes invaded the town of [Salmas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salmas) in northwestern [Persia](14653.md), massacring the Christian Armenian inhabitants after the withdrawal of Russian troops from the region. Prior to the Russian withdrawal, a large number of Christians fled across the [Aras river](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aras_\(river\)) into [Russia](25391.md), while a small number remained hidden in the homes of local Muslims.[^cite_note-323]

[Mohammad-Ali Jamalzadeh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammad-Ali_Jamalzadeh), a prominent Persian writer in the 20th century, studied in Europe where he joined a group of Iranian nationalists in Berlin who were to eventually start a newspaper (Rastakhiz) in Baghdad in 1915. During a trip from Baghdad to Istanbul he witnessed the deportations of Armenians and encountered many Armenian victims and corpses during his journey.[^cite_note-iranica-324] He wrote of his experiences and eyewitness accounts decades later in two books entitled "Qatl-e Amm-e Armanian" ([Persian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persian_language): قتل عام ارمنیان, literally; *Armenian massacres*) and "Qatl o ḡārat-e Arāmaneh dar Torkiye" (On the massacres of Armenians in Turkey).[^cite_note-iranica-324]

The current government of Iran has not officially acknowledged the Armenian genocide, partially due to geopolitical considerations around relations with Turkey. However [Armenia–Iran relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenia%E2%80%93Iran_relations) have been largely cordial and Iran is one of Armenia's major trade partners, as Turkey and Azerbaijan have blockaded the country.[^cite_note-325] Some Iranian politicians, such as the [reformist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_Reformists) president [Mohammad Khatami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammad_Khatami), who visited the Armenian genocide memorial in 2004, have paid respect to the victims of the Armenian genocide in the past.[^cite_note-326] When [principlist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_Principlists) president [Mahmoud Ahmadinejad](7907317.md) visited Armenia in 2007, he did not visit the memorial. When asked about it by a student at Yerevan university, he stated "Tehran's position on historical events is very clear, and we condemn all injustices throughout human history."[^cite_note-\:1-327] A few current and former members of the [Iranian parliament](383276.md) have also made statements indicating recognition of the genocide, including [Ali Akbar Mohtashamipour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ali_Akbar_Mohtashamipur), [Hadi Khamenei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hadi_Khamenei), and [Ruhollah Hosseinian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruhollah_Hosseinian), among others.[^cite_note-328][^cite_note-\:1-327]

In 2010, Turkey and Iran entered a political dispute after Iranian Vice President [Hamid Baghaei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamid_Baghaei) allegedly condemned Turkey for the Armenian genocide, while making a comparison related to the [WWII-era Invasion of Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anglo-Soviet_invasion_of_Iran). The tensions were resolved after the Iranian government issued a statement that his comments were taken out of context, and that Baghaei had only made a reference to the issue, without taking any position on the subject.[^cite_note-329] The Iranian [Ministry of Cultural Heritage, Handicrafts and Tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Cultural_Heritage,_Handicrafts_and_Tourism) also promptly issued a statement, saying Iran's official position on the Armenian genocide is identical to that of Turkey, which was followed by the Iranian embassy in [Ankara](802.md) stating the same.[^cite_note-330] In 2015, the [Iranian Armenian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_Armenians) community called on [Hassan Rouhani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hassan_Rouhani) to officially recognize the Armenian genocide, in order to prevent repeat occurrences of such events in the future.[^cite_note-331]

### United Kingdom

See also: [Armenian genocide denial § United Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_genocide_denial#United_Kingdom)

The [devolved legislatures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Devolution_in_the_United_Kingdom) of Scotland and Wales have formally recognized the Armenian genocide. The government of the United Kingdom does not recognize the Armenian genocide, as it considers that the evidence is not clear enough to retrospectively consider "the terrible events that afflicted the Ottoman Armenian population at the beginning of the last century" to be genocide under the 1948 UN convention. The British government states the "massacres were an appalling tragedy" and condemns them, stating that this was the view of the government during that period.[^cite_note-theguardian.com-233] In 2006, an [early day motion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Early_day_motion) recognizing the Armenian genocide by the UK Parliament was signed by 182 MPs.[^cite_note-332][^cite_note-333]



Armenian memorial unveiled in [Cardiff](5882.md) in 2007

However, in 2007, the position of the British government was that it condemns the massacres, but "neither this Government nor previous British Governments have judged that the evidence is sufficiently unequivocal to persuade us that these events should be categorised as genocide as defined by the [1948 UN Convention on Genocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Convention_on_the_Prevention_and_Punishment_of_the_Crime_of_Genocide), a convention which is, in any event, not retrospective in application."[^cite_note-334]

In 2009, the lawyer [Geoffrey Robertson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geoffrey_Robertson) QC revealed in a disclosure of Foreign Office documents entitled *"Was there an Armenian Genocide?"*,[^cite_note-Geoffrey-335] how the British Parliament has routinely been misinformed and misled by ministers who have recited [FCO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_and_Commonwealth_Office) briefs without questioning their accuracy. As summarized by Robertson, "there was no 'evidence' that had ever been looked at and there had never been a 'judgment' at all."[^cite_note-336]

A 1999 Foreign Office briefing for ministers said that the recognition of the Armenian genocide would provide no practical benefit to the UK and goes on to say that *"The current line is the only feasible option" owing to "the importance of our relations (political, strategic and commercial) with Turkey"*. The Foreign Office documents furthermore include advice from 1995 to the then Conservative foreign minister, [Douglas Hogg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Douglas_Hogg), that he should refuse to attend a memorial service for the victims of the genocide.[^cite_note-337] As of 2015, the United Kingdom does not formally recognize the Ottoman Empire's massacres of Armenians as a "genocide".[^cite_note-338]

[James Bryce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Bryce,_1st_Viscount_Bryce) (1838–1922), 1st Viscount Bryce, was one of the first Britons to bring this issue to public attention.[^cite_note-Travis-339][^cite_note-Travis2-340]

### Germany

See also: [Germany and the Armenian genocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Germany_and_the_Armenian_genocide)

Germany was an ally of the Ottoman Empire in World War I, and many German military officers were complicit in letting the genocide happen, and were also supporting the Ottoman Empire in fear that Armenians would collaborate with Russia at the time; several German figures like [Hans Humann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hans_Humann) and [Hans von Seeckt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hans_von_Seeckt) also demonstrated support for the Ottomans.[^cite_note-341][^cite_note-Dabag_pages_113-135-342] These actions were the seeds for the future [Holocaust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holocaust) by [Nazi Germany](21212.md) as many were inspired by the gruesome Armenian genocide.[^cite_note-Dabag_pages_113-135-342]

British journalist [Robert Fisk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Fisk), while acknowledging the role playing by most German diplomats and parliamentaries in the condemnation of the Ottoman Turks, noted that some of the German witnesses to the Armenian genocide would later go on to play a role in the Nazi regime. For example, [Konstantin Freiherr von Neurath](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Konstantin_Freiherr_von_Neurath), who was attached to the Turkish 4th Army in 1915 with instructions to monitor "operations" against the Armenians, later became [Adolf Hitler](2731583.md)'s foreign minister and "Protector of Bohemia and Moravia" during [Reinhard Heydrich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reinhard_Heydrich)'s terror in Czechoslovakia.[^cite_note-343]

Despite this, many other German officers had also openly disapproved the genocide and were appalled to see it happen. Count [Paul Wolff Metternich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_Wolff_Metternich) was an eyewitness who received numerous messages about the brutal Armenian genocide and deportation, and the Ottomans' determination to exterminate Armenians; his successor [Hans Freiherr von Wangenheim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hans_Freiherr_von_Wangenheim) also experienced similar story and documented the genocide.[^cite_note-Balakian-344][^cite_note-345] [Max Erwin von Scheubner-Richter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Max_Erwin_von_Scheubner-Richter), who documented various massacres of Armenians, had sent fifteen reports regarding "deportations and mass killings" to the German chancellery and detailed methods by the Ottoman government to commit atrocities and cover-up attempts by the Ottomans.[^cite_note-346]

German writer [Armin T. Wegner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armin_T._Wegner) had defied state censorship by taking photographs about the Armenian genocide.[^cite_note-347] He later stated: "I venture to claim the right of setting before you these pictures of misery and terror which passed before my eyes during nearly two years, and which will never be obliterated from my mind.".[^cite_note-348] He was eventually arrested by the Germans and recalled to Germany, but his work and open defiance toward the Ottoman censorship had made him a venerated name in Armenia for championing the cause in difficult times.[^cite_note-349]

Germany, in 2005, had decided to acknowledge the Armenian genocide for the first time.[^cite_note-350] The solution was later extended in 2016, causing tensions between Germany and Turkey to rise.[^cite_note-351]

### Austria

[Austria-Hungary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austria-Hungary) was an ally of the Ottoman Empire and ignored numerous requests for help from the [Armenian Catholicos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholicos_of_All_Armenians) in 1915. Reports about the genocide were suppressed by Markgraf Johann von Pallavicini (Austrian ambassador in Istanbul). Austrian newspapers kept silent about the genocide and adopted anti-Armenian stereotypes in their coverage of the defense of Van. The writer [Franz Werfel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franz_Werfel), in his book [The Forty Days of Musa Dagh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Forty_Days_of_Musa_Dagh), published in 1933, describes the beginning of the genocide from the perspective of the Armenians; it was later banned by the [Nazis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anschluss).[^cite_note-352] In 2015, the Austrian Parliament recognized and condemned the genocide of Armenian citizens. The Austrian government then called upon the government of Turkey to recognize the events of 1915–1916 as genocide. Turkey responded that they were 'disappointed' and implied Austria was presenting only the Armenian version of events.[^cite_note-\:4-73]

### China

Skepticism towards Turkey from China has resulted in a greater call for solidarity to the Armenian people in China, since relations between modern China and Armenia are friendly.[^cite_note-353] During the Armenian genocide, China hosted a small number of Armenian refugees, mostly concentrated around [Harbin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harbin) and [Tianjin](50821.md), prompting expressions of gratitude from the survivors.[^cite_note-354] Calls for recognition of the genocide in China also gather attention, notably in 2009 when the Turkish government blamed Chinese administration for the Uyghur–Han tensions, and in 2014 when a group of Armenian musicians authorized by Beijing performed classical Armenian musical pieces in commemoration of the centenary of the genocide in China.[^cite_note-355][^cite_note-356]

In 2011, erstwhile Chinese Foreign Minister [Yang Jiechi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yang_Jiechi) laid flowers on the Armenian genocide memorial in [Yerevan](34352.md).[^cite_note-357]

### Australia

[Australia](4689264.md) does not view the events at the end of the [Ottoman Empire](22278.md) as a genocide, although it does not deny it happened.[^cite_note-ata-a.org.au-358] Australia is one of the countries who were at war with the Ottoman Empire at the time of the events, notably during the [Gallipoli Campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gallipoli_Campaign). Australia does acknowledge the tragic events had devastating effects on the identity, heritage, and culture of all the people in the areas that the events have occurred. Furthermore, in response to the motions of [New South Wales](21654.md) and [South Australia](26716.md) to recognize the events as genocide, the Foreign Minister of Australia has clarified on June 4, 2014 that Australian states and territories have no constitutional role in the formulation of the Australian foreign policy, and that Australia does not view the tragic events at the end of the Ottoman Empire as a genocide.[^cite_note-ata-a.org.au-358]

### Israel

Until 2026, Israel neither recognized nor denied the Armenian genocide, a policy historically stemming from a few geopolitical considerations. According to *[The Times of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Times_of_Israel)*, "Israel is a small country in a hostile neighborhood that can't afford to antagonize the few friends it has in the region. Even more powerful states refuse to employ the 'genocide' term for fear of alienating Turkey...."[^cite_note-Ahren-359] Israel also shares what has been referred to as a "budding friendship" with [Azerbaijan](746.md), a "[Shiite Muslim](26961.md) but moderate country bordering Iran" that also strongly opposes recognition.[^cite_note-Ahren-359] According to former Israeli minister [Yossi Sarid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yossi_Sarid), one of the country's most vocal supporters of recognition, Israel tends to follow policies set by the United States, which had not recognized it.[^cite_note-Heller-360]

Despite these concerns, many prominent Israeli figures from across the political spectrum called for recognition.[^cite_note-Ahren-359][^cite_note-Heller-360] In 2003, the Catholicos of All Armenians [Karekin II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karekin_II) visited the then [Ashkenazi Chief Rabbi of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashkenazi_Chief_Rabbi_of_Israel) [Yona Metzger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yona_Metzger) who accepted an invitation to visit Armenia by Karekin II,[^cite_note-361] a trip that he made in 2005, including a visit to the [Tsitsernakaberd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tsitsernakaberd) (the Genocide Memorial in [Yerevan](34352.md)).[^cite_note-armenianchurch.org-362] While doing so he formally recognized the Armenian genocide as a historical fact.[^cite_note-ym-363] A 2007 survey found that more than 70% of Israelis thought that Israel should recognize the genocide, with 44% willing to break off relations with Turkey over the issue.[^cite_note-364]

Israel appeared to move closer to officially recognizing the genocide in 2011 when the [Knesset](74656.md) held its first open discussion on the matter. By a unanimous vote of 20–0, the Knesset approved referring the subject to the Education Committee for more extensive deliberation.[^cite_note-365] Israel's [speaker of the Knesset](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Knesset_speakers) told an Israel-based Armenian action committee that he intended to introduce an annual parliamentary session to mark the genocide.[^cite_note-366] A special parliamentary session held in 2012 to determine if Israel would recognize the Armenian genocide ended inconclusively. Then-Knesset speaker [Reuven Rivlin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reuven_Rivlin) and Cabinet Minister [Gilad Erdan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gilad_Erdan) were among those supporting formal recognition by the government.[^cite_note-367] The recognition was not approved at that time and in 2015, [Rafael Harpaz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafael_Harpaz), Israel's ambassador to Azerbaijan, said in an interview that Foreign Minister [Avigdor Lieberman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avigdor_Lieberman) has made it clear that Israel would not recognize the Armenian genocide, given Israel's hopes that its political and economic relationship with Turkey and later Azerbaijan could improve.[^cite_note-368][^cite_note-Patriarch-369]

[MKs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lists_of_Knesset_members) who support recognition have acknowledged the negative effect which it could have on [Israel–Azerbaijan](11716339.md) and [Israel–Turkey relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Turkey_relations). As then Knesset Spokesperson Rivlin said, "Turkey is and will be an ally of Israel. The talks with Turkey are understandable and even necessary from a strategic and diplomatic perspective. But those circumstances cannot justify the Knesset ignoring the tragedy of another people" and [Ayelet Shaked](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayelet_Shaked) (of the religious-nationalist [Jewish Home](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_Home) party) said: "We must confront our silence and that of the world in the face of such horrors."[^cite_note-haaretz517200-370][^cite_note-Ynetnews-371] Following the recognition of the genocide in 2026, the [Foreign ministry of Azerbaijan](11712667.md) called the recognition a "distortion of the historical facts".[^cite_note-372]

In 2000, then-education minister [Yossi Sarid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yossi_Sarid), chairman of the [Meretz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meretz) party, announced plans to place the Armenian genocide on Israel's history curricula.[^cite_note-Ahren-359] As a result of Sarid's 2000 speech to an Armenian church in Jerusalem recognizing their genocide, he became [persona non grata](521241.md) in Turkey.[^cite_note-Heller-360] Meretz has long fought for recognition of the genocide.[^cite_note-Patriarch-369] [Zehava Galon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zehava_Galon), a successor of Sarid as Meretz leader, has initiated several motions in the Knesset calling for the government to recognize the Armenian genocide. In 2013 she said, "Reconciliation with Turkey is an important and strategic move, but it should not affect the recognition."[^cite_note-Ynetnews-371] Her 2014 motion also enjoyed much support on the Knesset floor, including that of Knesset Speaker [Yuli Edelstein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yuli_Edelstein).[^cite_note-autogenerated1-373] Galon has also paid her respects at local Armenian memorial services.[^cite_note-374]

Rivlin, as [president of Israel](153049.md), was one of the Knesset's most outspoken proponents of recognition. It was reported in 2014 that due to the sensitivities of Israel's relationship with Turkey, Rivlin was quietly distancing himself from the Israeli campaign to recognize the Armenian genocide and chose not to sign the annual petition, which he had previously done.[^cite_note-375] Still, Israel has taken greater steps toward Armenian genocide recognition under Rivlin's presidency. Rivlin was the first Israeli president to speak at the United Nations on the issue.[^cite_note-Term-376] During the [United Nations Holocaust Memorial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Holocaust_Memorial) on January 28, 2015, President Rivlin spoke about the Armenian tragedy.[^cite_note-377] It was observed that in his speech he used the phrase רצח בני העם הארמני *reẓaḥ bnei haʿam haArmeni*, which means "the murder of the members of the Armenian nation," coming close to the Hebrew term for genocide, רצח עם *reẓaḥ ʿam*.[^cite_note-Skirts-378] In addition, 2015 marked the first time that Israel sent a delegation—[Nachman Shai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nachman_Shai) (Zionist Union) and [Anat Berko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anat_Berko) (Likud)—to Yerevan for the official memorial event.

&#x20;In an event billed as the first to be held by an Israeli president to commemorate the tragedy, Rivlin also invited Armenian communal and religious leader's to his official residence on April 26, 2015. He said we are "morally obligated to point out the facts, as horrible as they might be, we must not ignore them." As he skirted using the term "genocide," some Armenian leaders were disappointed, though still thankful for the event.[^cite_note-Skirts-378] However, it was later reported that earlier that same month, while briefing foreign journalists in English, Rivlin had in fact referred to the killings as genocide, saying:&#x20;

> It was [Avshalom Feinberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avshalom_Feinberg), one of my eldest brothers, who said 25 years before the Holocaust that if we do not warn against what is going on with the Armenians, what will happen afterwards when they try to do to us...? There is a saying that the Nazis used the Armenian genocide as something that gave them permission to bring the Holocaust into reality, according to their belief that they have to discriminate against the Jewish people. 'Never again' belongs to every one of you, all the nations. We cannot allow something like that to happen."[^cite_note-Term-376]

In 2015, a group of distinguished Israeli academics, artists, and former generals and politicians signed a [petition](http://www.combatgenocide.org/il/?page_id=4215) [Archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20150511165706/http://www.combatgenocide.org/il/?page_id=4215) May 11, 2015, at the [Wayback Machine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wayback_Machine) calling on Israel to follow the Pope's lead and recognize the genocide.[^cite_note-Heller-360] The signatories included author [Amos Oz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amos_Oz), historian [Yehuda Bauer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yehuda_Bauer), Major General (ret.) [Amos Yadlin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amos_Yadlin), former Likud minister [Dan Meridor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dan_Meridor) and about a dozen former MKs and ministers.[^cite_note-Ahren-359]

Rivlin visited the [Armenian Patriarchate of Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_Patriarchate_of_Jerusalem) on May 9, 2016. Concluding his speech he said, that "the Armenians were massacred in 1915. My parents remember thousands of Armenian migrants finding asylum at the Armenian Church. No one in Israel denies that an entire nation was massacred."[^cite_note-379]

On August 1, 2016, the [Knesset Committee on Education, Culture and Sports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Knesset_Committee_on_Education,_Culture_and_Sports) recognized the Armenian genocide.[^cite_note-380] In response to rising tensions between Turkey and Israel since the 2010s, the Israeli lobbies had refused to assist Turkey and Azerbaijan over the Armenian genocide dispute, which was partially responsible for the recognition of Armenian genocide by the U.S. Congress.[^cite_note-381]

In 2024, as tensions with Turkey escalated due to the [Gaza war](74998519.md), senior Israeli officials such as Prime Minister [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) and Foreign Minister [Israel Katz](19041378.md) mentioned the Armenian genocide in public statements criticizing Turkey.[^cite_note-382][^cite_note-383] In August 2025, Prime Minister Netanyahu said that he personally recognizes the Armenian genocide during an interview with conservative American businessman and podcaster [Patrick Bet-David](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patrick_Bet-David). *[Haaretz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haaretz)* noted that his statement does not constitute formal Israeli recognition.[^cite_note-384]

On June 28, 2026, the Israeli cabinet voted to formally recognize the genocide, following a resolution proposed by Foreign Minister [Gideon Sa'ar](485357.md), who called it a "moral and historical duty" and declared it is "never too late to do the right thing". The bill is still yet to be ratified by the Knesset.[^cite_note-\:5-163][^cite_note-385]

### Russia



Russian soldiers pictured in the Armenian village of Sheykhalan, 1915

The [Russian Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Empire) had fought in [World War I](4764461.md) as part of the Entente and thus it had conflicted with the Ottoman Empire. As such, Russian position toward the Armenian genocide was stemmed from the historical experience, where Russian troops frequently encountered deserted and destroyed villages, remains of Armenians, and mass atrocities committed against Armenian civilians by the Ottoman troops, which was reported by [Mikhail Papadyanov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mikhail_Papadyanov?action=edit\&redlink=1), Russian State Duma representative in [Baku](4566.md).[^cite_note-386][^cite_note-darbinyan-387] Russia was obliged to assist the Armenian civilians fleeing from genocide, and Russia had established humanitarian relief groups to deliver needed aids and supports for ethnic Armenians.[^cite_note-darbinyan-387] In 1916, shocking scenes obtained from [Erzurum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erzurum) led the Russians to retaliate against the Ottoman III Army whom they held responsible for the massacres, destroying it in its entirety.[^cite_note-388]

As for the result of this historical confrontation with the Ottomans, current poor relations between Russia and Turkey with regard to conflict in Syria, and its strong relations with the Armenians from 19th century onward, with Russia acting as a protector to the Christians including Armenians, Russia had acknowledged the Armenian genocide in 1995.[^cite_note-naryshkin-120][^cite_note-armenianow-121] In 2016, then-Prime Minister of Russia, [Dmitry Medvedev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dmitry_Medvedev), laid flowers in remembrance to the victims of Armenian genocide.[^cite_note-389]

### Japan

Japan was part of the Entente during World War I, but was not involved in the European front of the conflict as Japanese activities concentrated around China and other Asian territories. However, during the genocide, the Japanese had first reported about it and thus participated in the relief efforts to save the Armenian population. The effort was done by Viscount [Shibusawa Eiichi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shibusawa_Eiichi), who was alarmed by the ongoing massacre of the Armenian population.[^cite_note-390][^cite_note-391][^cite_note-392][^cite_note-393] However, as a result of its eventual role in [World War II](32927.md), [Japanese war crimes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_war_crimes) had been widely compared to the Ottoman war crimes; thus Japan has been reluctant to acknowledge the genocide due to fear of political backlash.[^cite_note-394]

### Finland and Sweden

[Finland](10577.md) has not recognized the Armenian genocide while Sweden recognized the genocide in 2010.[^cite_note-395][^cite_note-396] However, in August 2022 the [Left Youth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Left_Youth_\(Finland\)) Organization in Finland urged the [government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Finnish_Government) to recognize the Armenian genocide.[^cite_note-397] In June 2021, Finnish parliamentarian [Päivi Räsänen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/P%C3%A4ivi_R%C3%A4s%C3%A4nen) called on the government to recognize the Armenian genocide.[^cite_note-398] In September 2005, former Finnish president [Tarja Halonen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tarja_Halonen) visited [Tsitsernakaberd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tsitsernakaberd).[^cite_note-399] In February 2016, Swedish foreign minister [Margot Wallström](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Margot_Wallstr%C3%B6m) visited Tsitsernakaberd.[^cite_note-400]

### Poland











[Khachkars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khachkar) commemorating the genocide victims in [Kraków](16815.md), [Łódź](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%81%C3%B3d%C5%BA), [Gdańsk](12099.md), [Wrocław](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wroc%C5%82aw) and [Białystok](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bia%C5%82ystok)

Poland has historically strong relationships with both Armenia and Turkey. Historic Polish-Armenian contacts date back several centuries, with Poland being the home of a historically important [Armenian diaspora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenians_in_Poland) dating back to the [Late Middle Ages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Late_Middle_Ages), which in 2017 was commemorated by the [Senate of Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senate_of_Poland), which expressed gratitude for its contribution to Polish culture and history.
