The predecessor of modern Turkey, the Ottoman Empire, had rejected the [Partitions of Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Partitions_of_Poland), and many Poles had supported the Ottomans against Russia on their quest to regain independence from Russia, [Austria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austrian_Empire) and Germany.[^cite_note-EdukMK-401][^cite_note-402] Since the 19th century, both Armenia and Turkey were home to Polish refugees and exiles from [partitioned Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Partitions_of_Poland) (see [Polonezköy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polonezk%C3%B6y) and [Poles in Armenia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poles_in_Armenia)).[^cite_note-403]

Poland, a member of the European Union, supports Turkey to integrate further within the EU,[^cite_note-404] and supports Armenia's efforts for closer cooperation with the EU.[^cite_note-405]

Despite this strong Polish–Turkish relationship however, Poland officially recognized the Armenian genocide in 2005. Prominent Polish politicians such as [Lech Wałęsa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lech_Wa%C5%82%C4%99sa) had urged Turkey to acknowledge the genocide, where he made his speech in Echimadzin about the genocide, calling it "the first genocide of the 20th century",[^cite_note-406] although the claim is incorrect as the [Herero and Nama genocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Herero_and_Nama_genocide) of 1904–1908, also recognized by Poland, predates it.

There are several [khachkars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khachkars) in Poland, commemorating the Armenian genocide, e.g. in [Kraków](16815.md), [Wrocław](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wroc%C5%82aw),[^cite_note-407] [Łódź](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%81%C3%B3d%C5%BA),[^cite_note-408] [Gdańsk](12099.md), [Białystok](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bia%C5%82ystok),[^cite_note-409] [Gliwice](74600.md) and [Elbląg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elbl%C4%85g). In [Warsaw](32908.md), there is also a memorial to [Hasan Mazhar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hasan_Mazhar), Turkish governor of Ankara who refused to participate in the Armenian genocide.[^cite_note-psn-410]

### Romania and Moldova

Regarding [Romania](25445.md), in 2006, the [President](273659.md) [Traian Băsescu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traian_B%C4%83sescu) was asked if Romania would follow France and other Western states in recognizing the genocide. He then declared "we will not do anything affecting our neutrality in our relations with all the countries of the Black Sea region" and said Romania did not want to risk worsening relations with Turkey. Băsescu said Armenia was complicating Turkey's integration into the European Union by continuously raising the issue in the international community.[^cite_note-411] However, over the next several years, pressure for Romania to recognize the event grew. In 2016, [Cătălin Avramescu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C%C4%83t%C4%83lin_Avramescu?action=edit\&redlink=1), advisor to the then ex-president Băsescu, said "Romania has special duty to recognize Armenian Genocide";[^cite_note-412] while the Romanian-Armenian politician [Varujan Vosganian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Varujan_Vosganian), who is president of the [Union of Armenians of Romania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_of_Armenians_of_Romania), called on the [Romanian Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanian_Parliament) in 2019 to do the same.[^cite_note-413] In one poll, it was found that 72% of the Romanians surveyed were aware of the Armenian genocide.[^cite_note-414]

On the other hand, [Moldova](19260.md) has expressed greater tolerance for the possibility, which has even been considered in the [Moldovan Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moldovan_Parliament), although this is unlikely due to the good [relations between Moldova and Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moldova%E2%80%93Turkey_relations) and the desire not to deteriorate them.[^cite_note-415][^cite_note-416][^cite_note-417]

### Saudi Arabia

Historically, Saudi Arabia had backed Turkey over the Armenian genocide and was one of the few countries in the world to not acknowledge the independence of Armenia; Saudi Arabia also sided with Azerbaijan, the main ally of Turkey, over the [Nagorno-Karabakh conflict](4695860.md).[^cite_note-418][^cite_note-419] However, increasing tensions between Saudi Arabia and Turkey have resulted in Saudi Arabia slowly paying attention to the Armenian genocide, with government-run newspapers starting to mention the Armenian genocide, and anti-Turkish boycotts on the rise in the Saudi Kingdom as well.[^cite_note-420][^cite_note-421][^cite_note-judd-422]

In April 2019, Saudi Arabia supported an American Congressional resolution to recognize the Armenian genocide.[^cite_note-aravot-179] The Saudi Arabian ambassador in Lebanon had also paid a visit to the Armenian Genocide Memorial to demonstrate Saudi solidarity to Armenia.[^cite_note-armenpress-180] During the [2020 Nagorno-Karabakh conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Nagorno-Karabakh_conflict), Saudi Arabia had called for a boycott of Turkish goods after Turkish president Erdoğan blamed Saudi Arabia for the tensions in the Caucasus and the Middle East.[^cite_note-judd-422]

On the other hand, however, Saudi Arabia also needs to have leverage on relations with Azerbaijan, as Saudi Arabia has seen Azerbaijan as a potential place to destabilize [Iran](14653.md), an ally of Armenia but also Turkey and Saudi Arabia's adversary, thus Saudi Arabia takes its action carefully, although Saudi opposition to Turkey has soared.[^cite_note-423]

### India

India has neither officially recognized nor denied the Armenian genocide. However, during World War I, [the country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Raj) found itself at war against the [Ottoman Empire](22278.md), the predecessor of Turkey. Many [Armenians sought refuge in India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenians_in_India), most notably in [Kolkata](47905.md).[^cite_note-424] With the [relations between India and Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India%E2%80%93Turkey_relations) (and [effectively Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijan%E2%80%93India_relations)) worsened since the 2010s, mainly due to Turkey's (and effectively, Azerbaijan's) open support for [Pakistan](23235.md) (especially regarding the [Kashmir conflict](3030955.md)), which also shares similar stance (on the genocide) with Turkey and Azerbaijan, there has been growing call for recognition of the genocide in India.[^cite_note-425] For the first time, under the [Premiership of Narendra Modi](52507144.md), the Indian embassy in Armenia has mentioned the genocide and Indian ambassador Kishan Dan Dewal also paid respect to the victims of the genocide in 2021.[^cite_note-426]

### Other countries



Armenian genocide monument in [Larnaca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Larnaca), Cyprus. Cyprus was among the first countries to recognize the genocide.

[Azerbaijan](746.md), which is in a strategic alliance with Turkey and is in conflict with [Armenia](10918072.md) because of the [Nagorno-Karabakh conflict](4695860.md), shares the position of Turkey. During the Communist rule, when Azerbaijan was a part of the USSR, a brief description of the Armenian genocide was placed in the *[Azerbaijani Soviet Encyclopedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_Soviet_Encyclopedia)*.[^cite_note-427] Azerbaijan has actively lobbied against genocide recognition in other countries.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBen_Aharon2019346–347-428]

[Pakistan](23235.md) also supports the position of Turkey regarding the Armenian Genocide, with the Foreign Ministry calling U.S. recognition "one-sided and political".[^cite_note-429][^cite_note-430]

[Denmark](76972.md) believes (2008) that the genocide recognition should be discussed by historians, not politicians.[^cite_note-431] However, on January 26, 2017, the Danish Parliament adopted a resolution regarding the Armenian genocide, which recognizes the "tragic and bloody events that took place in eastern Anatolia in the period 1915–1923".[^cite_note-\:0-432]

In [Bulgaria](3415.md), activists first tried to persuade the parliament to acknowledge the genocide in 2008, but the proposal was voted down.[^cite_note-433] Shortly after the decision of the parliament, several of the biggest municipalities in Bulgaria accepted a resolution recognizing the genocide.[^cite_note-434] The resolution was first passed in [Plovdiv](182553.md) followed by [Burgas](86758.md), [Ruse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruse,_Bulgaria), [Stara Zagora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stara_Zagora), [Pazardzhik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pazardzhik) and others. In 2015, however, the [Bulgarian parliament](2122384.md) adopted a declaration recognizing the "mass extermination of the Armenian People in the Ottoman Empire" in the period 1915–1922, but did not use the word "genocide". This might be due to the [blackmailing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blackmail) Turkey did to Burgas, Haskovo and Svilengrad municipalities when they recognized the genocide.[^cite_note-standartnews.com-80][^cite_note-435][^cite_note-436]



The Armenian Heritage Park in downtown [Boston](24437894.md)

[Croatia](5573.md) and [Serbia](29265.md), two Balkan countries with shared Christian history to Armenia, have been reluctant to recognize the Armenian genocide due to economic relations with Turkey.[^cite_note-437] However, the two countries had fought brutal [Yugoslav Wars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yugoslav_Wars), both against and together toward the [Bosniaks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bosniaks), a South Slavic Muslim people with strong Turkish affinity. This resulted in the [Srebrenica massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Srebrenica_massacre) of 1995, where Serbian forces massacred 8,372[^cite_note-potocarimc.ba2-438] Bosniaks.

Turkey has recognized the Srebrenica massacre and has frequently used it to condemn other countries for "hypocrisies", as it did with the [Netherlands](21148.md) and [Vatican](32408.md), while Turkey at the same time openly denied the Armenian genocide.[^cite_note-439][^cite_note-440] Croatia and Serbia have both faced similar pressures from the Turkish government to not allow any official recognition of the Armenian genocide, while at the same time openly lambasting the Srebrenica massacre, which sometimes caused tensions between two Balkan nations to Turkey.[^cite_note-441][^cite_note-442][^cite_note-443][^cite_note-444]

In 2001, [Abd al-Qadir Qaddura](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abd_al-Qadir_Qaddura), speaker of the Syrian Parliament, became the first high-ranking Syrian official to acknowledge the Armenian genocide when he wrote in the Book of Remembrance of the Armenian Genocide Monument and Museum in Yerevan. Although Assad did not use the world genocide, two days after Assad's statement, [Bashar Jaafari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bashar_Jaafari), Syria's ambassador to the United Nations in Geneva, stated, "How about the Armenian genocide where 1.5 million people were killed?"[^cite_note-armenianweekly.com-445]

Deterioration of relations between [Egypt](8087628.md) and the [United Arab Emirates](69328.md) to Turkey had led to calls for recognition of Armenian genocide to grow in here as well. The [Emirate of Abu Dhabi](8821238.md) also in April 2019 had become the first place in the United Arab Emirates to openly acknowledge the Armenian genocide.[^cite_note-biyokulule-232] Earlier, in February 2019, the President of Egypt [Abdel Fattah el-Sisi](36703624.md) addressed his recognition of the Armenian genocide during the [2019 Munich Security Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/55th_Munich_Security_Conference)[^cite_note-egypttoday.com-157] and urged for complete recognition of the genocide by Egypt.[^cite_note-asbarez-158]

In 2023, Iraqi president [Abdul Latif Rashid](440441.md) visited the Armenian Genocide memorial in Yerevan and the official readout used the term genocide.[^cite_note-446][^cite_note-447]

## Armenian government

Historically, Armenia has consistently sought broad international recognition, but also adjusted how forcefully it pursues recognition in light of evolving diplomatic and strategic priorities. In 1995,[^cite_note-\:6-448] in 2009,[^cite_note-auto8-449][^cite_note-450][^cite_note-451] and in 2025,[^cite_note-452] the Armenian Government clarified that genocide recognition is not a precondition for normalizing relations with Turkey. Turkey made genocide recognition a precondition for lifting its contribution to the [Turkish-Azeri blockade of Armenia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish%E2%80%93Azeri_blockade_of_Armenia).[^cite_note-auto8-449]

In 2025, the prime minister of Armenia, [Nikol Pashinyan](17897961.md), questioned the historical context of the issue.[^cite_note-453][^cite_note-454][^cite_note-455] His attempts to revisit the history, meaning, and political framing of the Armenian Genocide have drawn intense criticism from scholars,[^cite_note-\:2-456][^cite_note-457] opposition figures, and diaspora institutions who argue that his rhetoric mirrors established [Turkish denialist narratives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_genocide_denial).[^cite_note-458][^cite_note-459][^cite_note-460][^cite_note-461]

Genocide scholar Suren Manukian stated that Pashinyan’s comments about the Armenian genocide revealed "a lack of elementary knowledge."[^cite_note-\:2-456] Former foreign minister [Vartan Oskanian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vartan_Oskanian) condemned Pashinyan for 'parroting' [Turkish ongoing denial of the genocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_genocide_denial).[^cite_note-\:2-456] Opposition leaders likewise asserted that the government has become "a collaborationist regime that serves only Turkey and Azerbaijan" and that Pashinyan’s approach represents "political immorality and betrayal" by treating the genocide as an "obstacle to peace."[^cite_note-462]

## Armenian diaspora and the genocide's recognition



Armenian Genocide memorial, [Kolkata](47905.md)



Armenian Genocide Remembrance Day, Kolkata



Commemoration of the Armenian Genocide, Armenian Church in [Volgograd](32585.md), [Russia](25391.md)



Armenian Genocide Memorial, [New Julfa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Julfa)

By the 1970s, [Armenian Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_Americans), who had accumulated wealth and political power, started efforts to lobby the U.S. government to recognize the genocide.[^cite_note-463]

The [Armenian National Committee of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_National_Committee_of_America) (ANCA) and the [Armenian Assembly of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_Assembly_of_America) (AAA) [lobby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_American_lobby) for recognition in the [United States Congress](31756.md), often in opposition to the [Turkish lobby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_lobby_in_the_United_States).[^cite_note-Cameron,_Fraser_pp.91-286][^cite_note-web.mit.edu-464]

During the later stages of the [Cold War](325329.md), fringe militant movements among Armenians arose. One of the goals of these Armenian militants was to agitate world governments for Turkish and international recognition of the Armenian genocide. Attacks on Turkish diplomats were committed in Europe, Asia and America. Two active groups which committed many of these attacks were the [Armenian Secret Army for the Liberation of Armenia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_Secret_Army_for_the_Liberation_of_Armenia) (ASALA) and the [Armenian Revolutionary Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_Revolutionary_Army) (ARA);[^cite_note-web.mit.edu-464] both organizations stopped their military activities in the late 1980s.

The [Armenian community in Kolkata, India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenians_in_India), numbering approximately 150 people,[^cite_note-465] travel to the St John's Church in [Chinsurah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hugli-Chuchura), annually, to observe Armenian Genocide Remembrance Day.[^cite_note-poulami-466] The community, along with visitors from countries such as Iran, Lebanon and Moscow held a memorial service at the 300-year-old [Armenian Holy Church of Nazareth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_Church_of_the_Holy_Nazareth) in Kolkata to mark the 100th anniversary of the genocide.[^cite_note-467] Armenian students at the [Jawaharlal Nehru University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jawaharlal_Nehru_University,_Delhi) in [New Delhi](51585.md) organized a candle lighting ceremony in the university campus and at the [Sacred Heart Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sacred_Heart_Cathedral,_New_Delhi) in the city to mark the anniversary. A commemoration ceremony was also held at the [Armenian Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_Church,_Chennai) in [Chennai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chennai).[^cite_note-468]

## Recent developments

### Since 2000

On March 29, 2000, the [Swedish parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swedish_parliament) approved a report recognizing the Armenian genocide and calling for Turkey's greater openness and an "unbiased independent and international research on the genocide committed against the Armenian people".[^cite_note-469] On June 12, 2008, the Swedish parliament voted by 245 to 37 (with 1 abstention, 66 absences) to reject a call for recognition of the 1915 genocide of the Ottoman Empire. On June 11 a long debate took place in the Swedish Parliament in regard to the Foreign Committee report on Human Rights, including five motions calling upon the Swedish Government and Parliament to officially recognize the genocide.[^cite_note-armtown-470]

The MPs adhered to the recommendation by the [Swedish Foreign Ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_for_Foreign_Affairs_\(Sweden\)) and Foreign Committee, arguing that there are "disagreements among scholars" in regard to the nature of the World War I events in Turkey, the non-retroactive nature of the UN Genocide Convention, and that the issue "should be left to historians". However, the Foreign Committee report stated that "the Committee understands that what happened to Armenians, Assyrians/Syriacs and Chaldeans during the Ottoman Empire's reign would probably be regarded as genocide according to the 1948 convention, if it had been in power at the time of the event".[^cite_note-471] Three days prior to the debate in the Parliament, a petition, signed by over 60 renowned genocide scholars, was published, calling on politicians in general, and Swedish parliamentarians in particular, not to abuse the name of science in denying a historic fact.[^cite_note-472] On March 11, 2010, the Swedish parliament recognized the genocide.[^cite_note-473]

On January 29, 2001, the [French Senate](567323.md) officially recognized the Armenian genocide.[^cite_note-auron-92]

In 2001, [Abd al-Qadir Qaddura](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abd_al-Qadir_Qaddura), speaker of the Syrian Parliament, became the first high-ranking Syrian official to acknowledge the Armenian genocide when he wrote in the Book of Remembrance of the Armenian Genocide Monument and Museum in Yerevan: "As we visit the Memorial and Museum of the Genocide that the Armenian nation suffered in 1915, we stand in full admiration and respect in front of those heroes that faced death with courage and heroism. Their children and grandchildren continued after them to immortalize their courage and struggle. ... With great respect we bow our heads in memory of the martyrs of the Armenian nation — our friends — and hail their ability for resoluteness and triumph. We will work together to liberate every human being from aggression and oppression."

On September 9, 2004, [President](233913.md) [Mohammad Khatami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammad_Khatami) of Iran visited the Armenian Genocide Memorial at [Tsitsernakaberd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tsitsernakaberd) in Yerevan.[^cite_note-474] On December 21, 2004, the [Dutch House of Representatives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/House_of_Representatives_\(Netherlands\)) officially recognized the Armenian genocide.[^cite_note-475] On April 19, 2005, the [Polish Parliament](28167.md) recognized the Armenian genocide.[^cite_note-asbarezP-118] On June 15, 2005, the German [Bundestag](3768.md) passed a resolution that "honors and commemorates the victims of violence, murder and expulsion among the Armenian people before and during the First World War". The [German resolution](http://dipbt.bundestag.de/dip21/btd/15/056/1505689.pdf) also states:

> The German parliament deplores the acts of the Government of the Ottoman Empire regarding the almost complete destruction of Armenians in Anatolia and also the inglorious role of the German Reich in the face of the organized expulsion and extermination of Armenians which it did not try to stop. Women, children and elderly were from February 1915 sent on death marches towards the Syrian desert.

The expressions "organized expulsion and extermination" resulting in the "almost complete destruction of Armenians" is sufficient in any language to amount to formal recognition of the Armenian genocide, although of course the crime of 'genocide' had not been legally defined in 1915. The Resolution also contains an apology for German responsibility as a then ally of Turkey.[^cite_note-bundestag-476][^cite_note-Deutshe_Welle-477] On September 4, 2006, [Members of the European Parliament](258909.md) voted for the inclusion of a clause prompting Turkey "to recognize the Armenian genocide as a condition for its EU accession" in a highly critical report, which was adopted by a broad majority in the foreign relations committee of the [European Parliament](9581.md).[^cite_note-478][^cite_note-479] This requirement was later dropped on September 27, 2006, by the general assembly of the European Parliament by 429 votes in favor to 71 against, with 125 abstentions.[^cite_note-480] In dropping the pre-condition of acceptance of the Armenian genocide, (which could not be legally demanded of Turkey), The European Parliament said: "MEPs nevertheless stress that, although the recognition of the Armenian genocide as such is formally not one of the Copenhagen criteria, it is indispensable for a country on the road to membership to come to terms with and recognize its past."

On September 26, 2006, the two largest political parties in the Netherlands, [Christian Democratic Appeal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_Democratic_Appeal) (CDA) and the [Labour Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labour_Party_\(Netherlands\)) (PvdA), removed three Turkish-Dutch candidates for the [2006 general election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dutch_general_election,_2006), because they either denied or refused to publicly declare that the Armenian genocide had happened. The magazine *[HP/De Tijd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HP/De_Tijd)* reported that the number 2 of the PvdA list of candidates, [Nebahat Albayrak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nebahat_Albayrak) (who was born in Turkey and is of Turkish descent) had acknowledged that the term "genocide" was appropriate to describe the events. Albayrak denied having said this and accused the press of putting words in her mouth, saying that "I'm not a politician that will trample my identity. I've always defended the same views everywhere with regard to the 'genocide'".[^cite_note-481] It was reported that a large section of the Turkish minority were considering boycotting the elections.[^cite_note-482] [Netherlands' Turkish minority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turks_in_the_Netherlands) numbers 365,000 people, out of which 235,000 are eligible to vote.

On November 29, 2006, the lower house of Argentina's parliament adopted a resolution recognizing the Armenian genocide. The bill was overwhelmingly adopted by the assembly and declared April 24, the international day of remembrance for the Armenian genocide as an official "day of mutual tolerance and respect" among peoples around the world.

On April 20, 2007, the [Basque Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basque_Parliament) approved an institutional declaration recognizing the Armenian genocide. The Basque Parliament included six articles where it affirms the authenticity of the Armenian genocide and declares sympathy to the Armenians, while at the same time denouncing Turkey's negation of the genocide and its economic blockade imposed on Armenia.[^cite_note-483]



Former Russian President [Dmitry Medvedev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dmitry_Medvedev) laying a wreath at the Armenian Genocide Memorial, October 2008

On June 5, 2007, the [Chilean Senate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chilean_Senate) unanimously adopted legislation recognizing the Armenian genocide and urging its government to support a key 1985 United Nations Subcommission report describing this crime against humanity as a clear instance of genocide.[^cite_note-484]

On November 23, 2007, the [Mercosur](25869317.md) parliament adopted a resolution recognizing the "Armenian Genocide, perpetrated by the Ottoman Empire, which took 1.5 million lives from 1915 to 1923". The Mercosur resolution also expressed its support for the Armenian Cause and called on all countries to recognize the genocide.[^cite_note-485]

### Since 2010

On March 5, 2010, the [Catalan](6822.md) Parliament recognized the Armenian genocide on the initiative of the members of [Barcelona](4443.md)'s Friendship Union with Armenia.[^cite_note-Catalonia-184]

On March 11, 2010, the Swedish parliament voted to describe the massacre of Armenians by the Ottoman Empire in 1915 as genocide. The resolution was adopted with 131 deputies voting in favor of the resolution and 130 voting against it.

On March 25, 2010, the [Serbian Radical Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbian_Radical_Party) submitted a draft resolution to the Serbian parliament condemning the genocide committed by Ottoman Turkey against Armenians from 1915 to 1923. SRS submitted the draft so that Serbia can join the countries which have condemned the genocide.[^cite_note-486] As of March 10, 2014, Serbia does not recognize the events as a genocide,[^cite_note-487] thus it can be said the draft has failed.

On February 17, 2011, Chinese foreign minister [Yang Jiechi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yang_Jiechi) paid tribute to the memory of the Armenian genocide victims during his visit to Armenia. He also met with the head of Armenia's national church, Catholicos of All Armenians [Karekin II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karekin_II), and discussed the recognition of the Armenian genocide.[^cite_note-488]

During his meeting with Armenia's President [Serzh Sargsyan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serzh_Sargsyan) in January 2014, the Czech President [Miloš Zeman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milo%C5%A1_Zeman) stated: "Next year marks the 100th anniversary of the Armenian genocide. In 1915, 1.5 million Armenians were killed."[^cite_note-489]

In 2014 Syrian president [Bashar al-Assad](364813.md) became the first Syrian head of state to acknowledge the mass murders of Armenians and identify the perpetrator as Ottoman Turkey, stating, "The degree of savagery and inhumanity that the terrorists have reached reminds us of what happened in the Middle Ages in Europe over 500 years ago. In more recent modern times, it reminds us of the massacres perpetrated by the Ottomans against the Armenians, when they killed a million and a half Armenians and half a million Orthodox Syriacs in Syria and in Turkish territory." Although Assad did not use the world genocide, two days after Assad's statement, [Bashar Jaafari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bashar_Jaafari), Syria's ambassador to the United Nations in Geneva, stated, "How about the Armenian genocide where 1.5 million people were killed?"[^cite_note-armenianweekly.com-445]

On June 20, 2014, the [Presbyterian Church (USA)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presbyterian_Church_\(USA\)) adopted a resolution recognizing the Armenian genocide and adopted the 2015 church calendar designating April 26 as the day for its observance.[^cite_note-theorthodoxchurch.info-490][^cite_note-mirrorspectator.com-491] It also directed the church's Mission Agency to prepare educational and liturgical resources for member churches in preparation for this event.[^cite_note-mirrorspectator.com-491] This resolution was the first of its kind for a major American church body.[^cite_note-mirrorspectator.com-491]

On June 23, 2014, the parliament of [Spain](26667.md)'s autonomous community of [Navarre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Navarre) adopted a measure recognizing the Armenian genocide. The measure came after a series of visits by members of the Navarre parliament to Armenia and Artsakh.[^cite_note-492]

On November 26, 2014, the Plurinational Legislative Assembly of [Bolivia](3462.md) unanimously passed a resolution in solidarity with the claims of the Armenian people and condemning "all denialist policy regarding the genocide and crimes against humanity suffered by the Armenian nation."[^cite_note-493]

On March 11, 2015, American actor [George Clooney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Clooney) showed solidarity and support in a day of remembrance marking the 100th anniversary of the Armenian Genocide at an event in New York City. Clooney took to the stage and spoke about the significance of the centennial and of recognizing the genocide. Clooney was joined by his wife [Amal Clooney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amal_Clooney), who went before Europe's top human rights court in January, representing Armenia, to argue against a man convicted of denying the 1915 Armenian Genocide.[^cite_note-494]

On March 17, 2015, the [People's Council of Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Council_of_Syria) — during a session dedicated to the 100th anniversary of the genocide — condemned the [Ottoman Empire](22278.md) for its responsibility for the Armenian genocide. The speaker of the parliament [Mohammad Jihad al-Laham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammad_Jihad_al-Laham) issued a statement on behalf of the members, where he expressed solidarity with the Armenians worldwide in their struggle for justice.[^cite_note-495]

On March 27, 2015, Armenian and Greek youth held a protest in the Greek capital of [Athens](1216.md). Protesters demanded that [European Union member states](10890716.md) end denial policies, recognize the Armenian genocide, and subject deniers to criminal liability. The protesters further called on the European Union to pressure Turkey to recognize the genocide and take action to compensate for the material and non-material losses and restoration of the historic rights of the Armenian nation.[^cite_note-496]

On April 2, 2015, American band [System of a Down](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/System_of_a_Down) embarked on their [Wake Up The Souls Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wake_Up_The_Souls_Tour), which kicked off April 6 and culminated with the band's first performance in Armenia's capital city of Yerevan on April 23. "The goal is to raise awareness about the Armenian genocide, and also to put the idea into people's minds that justice can prevail, even if it's been a hundred years," said band member [Serj Tankian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serj_Tankian). The group visited a total of 14 cities across the world.[^cite_note-497]

On April 4, 2015, forty-nine members of the [United States House of Representatives](19468510.md) wrote a letter to U.S. President [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama) requesting a "full and just acknowledgment of the Armenian Genocide", claiming that the move would help improve [Armenia–Turkey relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenia%E2%80%93Turkey_relations).[^cite_note-498]

On April 6, 2015, Armenian-American reality stars [Kim Kardashian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kim_Kardashian) and [Khloé Kardashian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khlo%C3%A9_Kardashian) traveled to Armenia. During their stay, the sisters visited the [Tsitsernakaberd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tsitsernakaberd) Genocide Memorial in Yerevan and in doing so, brought global media attention to the recognition of the Armenian genocide. Kim Kardashian's husband [Kanye West](523032.md) also visited Armenia.[^cite_note-499]

On April 7, 2015, [Ngāpuhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ng%C4%81puhi) leader David Rankin called for [Māori people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%C4%81ori_people) to boycott the centennial [ANZAC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ANZAC) commemorations because the Turkish Government was using the event to deflect attention from the Armenian genocide. Mr Rankin stated that "the Armenian population was slaughtered by the colonizing Turks and our involvement in the ANZAC centennial at Gallipoli is supporting the genocide of the colonizer." Mr Rankin called on [Maori](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%C4%81ori_people) of [New Zealand](4913064.md) and other indigenous groups to boycott the ANZAC Day events and, as a sign of their solidarity with the Armenians, to stop wearing poppies.[^cite_note-500] The [Green Party of Aotearoa New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_Party_of_Aotearoa_New_Zealand) also supported the move to recognize the Armenian genocide. Green Party human rights spokesperson [Catherine Delahunty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catherine_Delahunty) stated that New Zealand should be using its spot on the [United Nations Security Council](31956.md) to advocate for genocide recognition.[^cite_note-501]

On April 12, 2015, [Pope Francis](1687680.md) described the massacres of 1.5 million Armenians in Ottoman Turkey as "the first genocide of the 20th century" during an unprecedented Vatican Mass dedicated to the 100th anniversary of the tragedy. Francis remembered the victims of "that immense and senseless slaughter" at the start of the Mass at [St. Peter's Basilica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Peter's_Basilica), which was attended by President Serzh Sarkisian, the supreme heads of the Armenian Apostolic Churches and hundreds of Armenian Catholics.[^cite_note-502] Turkey responded by recalling its ambassador to the [Holy See](13393.md).[^cite_note-503]

On April 14, 2015, the Parliament of [Corsica](5714828.md) adopted a resolution recognizing the fact of the Armenian genocide. With this resolution, the [Corsican Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corsican_Assembly) urged Turkey to recognize the Genocide and normalize relations with Armenia. The resolution was introduced by the [Femu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Femu_a_Corsica), a Corsican political coalition.[^cite_note-504]

On April 15, 2015, the [European Parliament](9581.md) adopted a resolution by a majority vote that called the massacre a century ago of up to 1.5 million Armenians by Ottoman Turkish forces a genocide. The motion, which garnered support from all political groups, encouraged Turkey to "use the commemoration of the centenary of the Armenian genocide as an important opportunity" to open its archives, "come to terms with its past" as well as recognize the genocide. By doing so, Turkey would pave the way for a "genuine reconciliation between the Turkish and Armenian peoples". The document calls on Turkey to restore its diplomatic ties with Armenia, open the border and strive for economic integration. The European Parliament further called on all European Union member states to recognize the Armenian genocide.[^cite_note-505]

On April 17, 2015, Armenian religious and secular organizations in [Georgia](48768.md) petitioned the country's parliament to begin formal debates on the recognition of the Armenian genocide.[^cite_note-506] On April 24, 2015, thousands marched in the province of [Javakheti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javakheti) demanding that Turkey recognize the genocide. Another rally took place near the Turkish embassy in [Tbilisi](42298.md).[^cite_note-507]

On April 18, 2015, the [Austrian People's Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austrian_People's_Party) and the [Social Democratic Party of Austria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_Democratic_Party_of_Austria) presented a resolution that condemns the Armenian genocide to the Parliament of [Austria](26964606.md).[^cite_note-508] On April 21, 2015, the Austrian parliament officially adopted a statement condemning the Armenian genocide and called on Turkey to face its past. All six factions of the Austrian parliament signed up to the statement that also emphasizes the responsibility of [Austria-Hungary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austria-Hungary), as an ally of the Ottoman Empire during World War I, in the Armenian genocide.[^cite_note-509]

On April 20, 2015, German Chancellor [Angela Merkel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angela_Merkel)'s spokesman [Steffen Seibert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steffen_Seibert) said the government of [Germany](11867.md) would support a resolution in parliament on Friday declaring the Armenian genocide an example of genocide. Chancellor Angela Merkel and her coalition, including the [Christian Democratic Union of Germany](60584.md) voted on April 24 to label the murders as genocide as defined by the United Nations in 1948. The lower house vote was, on the same day as leaders met in the Armenian capital of [Yerevan](34352.md), to commemorate the massacre that began in April 1915. Germany has been under pressure from some of its European partners to follow their example and more fully recognize the depth of the Armenian tragedy.[^cite_note-510]

On April 21, 2015, members of the Serbian opposition political group [New Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Party_\(Serbia\)), submitted a draft resolution on the recognition and condemnation of genocide perpetrated by the Ottoman Empire to the Parliament of [Serbia](29265.md). New Party called on the Serbian government and citizens to observe April 24 as a day of remembrance of the victims of the Armenian genocide. The draft resolution also called on other countries, including Turkey, and international organizations to recognize and condemn the genocide against the Armenian people in the hopes of preventing such crimes from happening in the future.[^cite_note-511]

On April 22, 2015, Israeli President [Reuven Rivlin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reuven_Rivlin) spoke out strongly on the question of the Armenian genocide in a closed session with journalists in [Jerusalem](16043.md). Rivlin drew a direct historical link between the world's failure to prevent the Armenian genocide and the Holocaust. "The Nazis," he said, "used the Armenian genocide as something that gave them permission to bring the Holocaust into reality."[^cite_note-512]

On April 22, 2015, the President of Armenia, Serzh Sargsyan expressed his gratitude to the People's Council of Syria for its steps aimed at the recognition of the Armenian genocide. The speaker of the People's Council of the [Syrian Arab Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_Arab_Republic), Mohammad Jihad al-Laham, stated that Syria recognizes the Armenian genocide committed by the Ottoman Empire.[^cite_note-513]

On April 23, 2015, Russian President [Vladimir Putin](32817.md) described the 1915 Armenian massacres in Ottoman Turkey as genocide ahead of his participation in upcoming official ceremonies in Yerevan to mark the 100th anniversary of the tragedy. "One century on, we bow our heads in memory of all victims of this tragedy which our country has always perceived as its own pain and calamity," he said.[^cite_note-514]

On April 23, 2015, the [Flemish Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flemish_Parliament) of [Belgium](3343.md) unanimously adopted a resolution to fully recognize the Armenian genocide. The motion was put forward by all political parties of [Flanders](10878.md), including the [Christen-Democratisch en Vlaams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christen-Democratisch_en_Vlaams) party. The motion further called on Turkey to recognize the massacres as a genocide.[^cite_note-515]

On April 24, 2015, the President of Serbia, [Tomislav Nikolić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tomislav_Nikoli%C4%87), stated that the people of [Serbia](29265.md) realize what happened to the Armenians in the Ottoman Empire and that a monstrous genocide did occur. The President, along with other world leaders, travelled to the Armenian capital of Yerevan to commemorate the centennial of the genocide. "The denial of historical truth adversely affects the level of awareness," the Serbian president stressed, and added: "We have not come here to be against or for someone, but to honor the memory of the victims of the people of Armenia."[^cite_note-516]

On April 24, 2015, in [Los Angeles](18110.md), more than 130,000 people took part in the [March for Justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Armenian_March_for_Justice) to mark the centennial of the Armenian genocide. Demonstrators started the march from the Little Armenia neighborhood and proceeded to walk 10 km (6 mi) to the Turkish consulate. Demonstrators demanded that the killing of around 1.5 million Armenians by Ottoman Turks in 1915 be recognized as a genocide.

On April 24, 2015, the lights of the [Eiffel Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eiffel_Tower) in [Paris](22989.md) and the [Coliseum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coliseum) in [Rome](25458.md) went dark in recognition and remembrance of the Armenian genocide. Several media outlets reported that Europe stood with Armenia at this time.[^cite_note-517]

On April 25, 2015, in Uruguay, President [Tabaré Vázquez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tabar%C3%A9_V%C3%A1zquez), Vice-president [Raúl Fernando Sendic Rodríguez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ra%C3%BAl_Fernando_Sendic_Rodr%C3%ADguez), and other political leaders recognized the centennial at an event in [Montevideo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montevideo). Speakers pushed for further recognition and criticized Turkey's denialism.[^cite_note-518]

Throughout April 2015, several American states adopted resolutions on the occasion of the Armenian genocide centennial. [Wisconsin](33127.md) commemorated the "Day of Remembrance for the 100th Anniversary of the Armenian genocide of 1915 to 1923 with a joint resolution. [Pennsylvania](23332.md) unanimously passed H.R. 265 designating April 24, 2015, as "Pennsylvania's Day of Remembrance of the 100th anniversary of the Armenian genocide. [Tennessee](30395.md) passed HR 100 designating April 24 as the official day of remembrance for the Armenian genocide. The [New York State Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_State_Assembly) passed Res.374 "intended to counter the tide of revisionist history which purports that the Armenian Genocide never took place." The [Georgia House of Representatives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Georgia_House_of_Representatives) passed Resolution H.R. 904 which declares April 24 as Armenian Genocide Remembrance Day.[^cite_note-519] As of April 2015, 44 out of 50 U.S. states have, by legislation or proclamation, recognized the Armenian genocide.[^cite_note-520]

On April 27, 2015, a call to recognize the killing of an estimated 1.5 million Armenians as "genocide" was supported by the leaders of the main Irish churches. Clergy from seven Christian denominations and a representative of the Jewish Community were among those calling on the [Republic of Ireland](14560.md) to recognize the genocide. Roman Catholic auxiliary bishop of Dublin [Raymond Field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raymond_Field_\(bishop\)) said to the Armenians "I stand in solidarity with you, and we share your pain and sadness." [Church of Ireland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_Ireland) [Archbishop of Dublin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archbishop_of_Dublin_\(Church_of_Ireland\)) Dr [Michael Jackson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Jackson_\(bishop\)) also described the killing as a "genocide" which demanded "recognition and response".[^cite_note-521] On the same day, Armenia called on Ireland to recognize the genocide, the call was made by the Armenian Consul to Ireland.[^cite_note-522]

On April 29, 2015, members of the [Kurdistan Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurdistan_Parliament) in [Iraq](7515928.md) submitted a legislative proposal to recognize the events of 1915 as the Armenian genocide. The [Patriotic Union of Kurdistan](70789.md) extended its support of the proposal. The draft law also proposes to declare April 24 as an official non-working day in [Iraqi Kurdistan](679693.md).[^cite_note-523]

On May 30, 2015, the [Young European Socialists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Young_European_Socialists) gathered in Riga, [Latvia](17514.md) where a resolution recognizing the Armenian genocide was passed. The group also condemned Turkey's denial of the crime and called on Turkey to begin a process of reparations for the genocide.[^cite_note-524]

On June 2, 2015, the Federal Senate of [Brazil](3383.md) passed a resolution recognizing the Armenian genocide. The resolution expresses its "solidarity with the Armenian people during the course of the centenary of the campaign of extermination of its population" and states that "the Senate recognizes the Armenian genocide, whose centenary was commemorated on April 24, 2015."[^cite_note-525]

On October 29, 2015, the Senate of the Republic of [Paraguay](23420.md) unanimously approved an official recognition of the Armenian genocide. "The Senate of the Republic of Paraguay recognizes the genocide of the Armenian people in the period 1915-1923, committed by the Turkish-Ottoman Empire, when commemorating this year the centenary of that crime against humanity," reads Article 1 of the statement presented by the Progressive Democratic Party.[^cite_note-526]

On November 30, 2015, the [Parliament of South Ossetia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliament_of_South_Ossetia) considered the issue of recognition of the Armenian genocide, as announced by Speaker [Anatoly Bibilov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anatoly_Bibilov). "We regret that South Ossetia has not recognized the Armenian Genocide until now. The issue is on the parliament agenda now, and will be put up for discussion. It's necessary to give a proper assessment of the crime committed in the Ottoman Empire and condemn the policy of denial of genocide," he said. He added that South Ossetia is not afraid of damaging ties with Turkey, as there are no relations as such.[^cite_note-527]

On June 2, 2016, [Germany](11867.md)'s [Bundestag](3768.md) passed a resolution recognizing the Armenian genocide and admitting its part of responsibility for it, as the main ally of the Ottoman Empire during World War I, which led to a furious reaction in Turkey.[^cite_note-528] Turkish president [Recep Tayyip Erdoğan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recep_Tayyip_Erdo%C4%9Fan) said German lawmakers of Turkish origin who voted for the resolution have "tainted blood" and that their blood "must be tested in a lab." The president of Germany's parliament, [Norbert Lammert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norbert_Lammert) said he was shocked that threats against the parliamentarians had been backed by high-ranking politicians, and said parliament would respond with all legal options. Also, [Martin Schulz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Schulz), a member of the [Social Democratic Party of Germany](60585.md) (SPD) and president of the European Parliament, condemned Erdoğan's comments.[^cite_note-529] The German foreign ministry had warned lawmakers with Turkish origin against travel to Turkey because their safety could not be guaranteed and also they received increased police protection and further security measures for both their professional and private activities.[^cite_note-530]

On June 24, 2016, [Pope Francis](1687680.md) in a speech described the killing of Armenians as a genocide. Also, the Vatican spokesman [Federico Lombardi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federico_Lombardi), told the reporters that "There is no reason not to use this word in this case," "The reality is clear and we never denied what the reality is." Turkey condemned the declaration as "very unfortunate" and also said that it bore traces of "the mentality of the Crusades."[^cite_note-531][^cite_note-532]

On July 1, 2016, [France](5843419.md)'s lower house of parliament unanimously voted to criminalize the denial of all crimes against humanity. The amendment covered all events which the French law defined as genocide, crimes against humanity, war crimes or slavery, including the Armenian genocide. The law set out penalties of up to one year in prison and a 45,000 € (\$50,000) fine for those who contradicted it. It is yet to be passed by France's Senate.[^cite_note-533][^cite_note-534][^cite_note-auto1-535] The Turkish Foreign Ministry said that this law is a risk to freedom of expression[^cite_note-auto1-535]

On August 1, 2016, the [Knesset](74656.md)'s Education, Culture and Sports Committee announced its recognition of the Armenian genocide and urged the Israeli government to formally acknowledge the 1915 mass slaughter of 1.5 million Armenians as such.[^cite_note-536]

On September 17, 2016, the [Andean Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andean_Parliament), a legislative body composed of representatives of [Bolivia](3462.md), [Colombia](5222.md), [Ecuador](9334.md), [Peru](170691.md) and [Chile](5489.md), approved a resolution recognizing the "Armenian Genocide perpetrated by the authorities of the Ottoman Empire between the years 1915–1923" and condemning "any policy of denial with respect to genocide and crimes against humanity suffered by the Armenian nation."[^cite_note-537]

On October 14, 2016, the [French Senate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Senate) — the upper house of the Parliament of [France](5843419.md) — approved a draft law criminalizing the denial of the Armenian genocide. The bill, which had been unanimously passed by the lower house of the French parliament on July 1, set out penalties of up to one year in prison and a 45,000 € (US\$50,000) fine for those who publicly deny the genocide.[^cite_note-538] The law was later overturned by the [Constitutional Council](1141259.md), which ruled that it represented an unconstitutional "blow to the freedom of expression which is neither necessary nor proportionate."[^cite_note-539] As of 2021, Armenian genocide denial is not a criminal offence in France.

On January 26, 2017, the Parliament of [Denmark](76972.md) approved a resolution that condemns Turkish legislation for banning citizens and mass media from using the term 'genocide' and that it is an unreasonable restriction on academic freedom and freedom of speech. The resolution also condemned the acts of violence committed against the Armenian people.[^cite_note-540]

In a letter dated April 21, 2017, and addressed to the Armenian National Committee of America Western Region (ANCA-WR), Wyoming Governor [Matt Mead](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matt_Mead) has recognized the Armenian genocide and praised the work of Armenian American grassroots. [Wyoming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wyoming) became the 45th U.S. state to recognize the Armenian genocide.[^cite_note-541]

On May 19, 2017, the [Texas House of Representatives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_House_of_Representatives) unanimously passed House Resolution 191, titled "Recognizing the Armenian Genocide". [Texas](29810.md) became the 46th U.S. state to recognize the Armenian genocide.[^cite_note-542]

On August 24, 2017, [Iowa](26810748.md) Governor [Kim Reynolds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kim_Reynolds) signed a proclamation memorializing the Armenian Genocide and declaring October 2017 as "Armenia Awareness Month" in Iowa, making it the 47th U.S. state to officially recognize the Armenian genocide.[^cite_note-543]

On November 6, 2017, [Indiana](21883857.md) Governor [Eric Holcomb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eric_Holcomb) issued a powerful proclamation memorializing the Ottoman Turkish Empire's centrally planned and executed annihilation of close to three million Armenians, Greeks, Assyrians, and Syriacs, making the Hoosier State the 48th U.S. state to properly recognize and condemn the Armenian genocide.[^cite_note-ReferenceA-239]

On February 22, 2018, the parliament of the [Netherlands](21148.md) adopted two resolutions on the Armenian genocide with an absolute majority of votes. The first resolution reaffirms the decision of the Netherlands in 2004 to recognize the Armenian genocide, while by the second resolution, the foreign minister of the Netherlands will be obliged to visit [Armenia](10918072.md) and pay tribute to the victims of the Armenian genocide at Tsitsernakaberd memorial complex every 5 years.[^cite_note-544]

Due to the ongoing deterioration of relations between Egypt and Turkey, the Government of Egypt, led by el-Sisi, has been receiving proposals to recognize the Armenian genocide, a sensitive subject which Turkey has many times denounced.[^cite_note-545] Filmmaker Mohamed Hanafi had produced a movie, "Who Killed the Armenians?", in response to ongoing tensions between Turkey and Egypt, as an act of Egyptian solidarity to Armenia.[^cite_note-546] In February 2019, Abdel Fattah el-Sisi announced it had implicitly recognized the Armenian genocide, further deteriorating the [relationship between Turkey and Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egypt%E2%80%93Turkey_relations).[^cite_note-egypttoday.com-157]

On February 5, 2019, French President Emmanuel Macron declared April 24 as Armenian genocide commemoration day in [France](5843419.md).[^cite_note-News.am-93]

On March 20, 2019, [Alabama](303.md) became the 49th US state to formally recognize the Armenian genocide.[^cite_note-547]

On April 10, 2019, the executive committee of the [Centrist Democrat International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centrist_Democrat_International) political alliance met in Brussels where participants officially adopted the resolution recognizing and condemning the Armenian genocide.[^cite_note-548] On April 10, 2019, the [Italian Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chamber_of_Deputies_\(Italy\)) officially recognized the Armenian genocide.[^cite_note-549] On April 26, 2019, the [Portuguese Parliament](1123482.md) officially recognized the Armenian genocide.[^cite_note-550] On October 29, 2019, the [US House of Representatives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_House_of_Representatives) passed a resolution on a 405–11 vote to recognize the Armenian genocide.[^cite_note-\:3-551] Moreover, the House backed a legislation calling on President [Donald Trump](4848272.md) to impose sanctions on Turkey,[^cite_note-552] following the [Turkish offensive into north-eastern Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Turkish_offensive_into_north-eastern_Syria).[^cite_note-553] On December 12, 2019, the [US Senate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_Senate) passed unanimously a resolution to recognize the genocide.[^cite_note-554]

### Since 2020

On April 16, 2021, the [Legislative Assembly of Alberta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legislative_Assembly_of_Alberta) in [Canada](5042916.md) unanimously passed a law which recognizes the Armenian genocide and [other genocides](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genocides_in_history), and the government declared that the month of April is Genocide Remembrance, Condemnation and [Prevention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genocide_prevention) Month.[^cite_note-555]

On April 24, 2021, Vice President of the European Parliament [Fabio Massimo Castaldo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fabio_Massimo_Castaldo) called on the [27 EU member states](10890716.md) to recognize and strongly condemn the Armenian genocide.[^cite_note-556]

On April 24, 2021, President [Joe Biden](145422.md) officially recognized the Armenian genocide, thus, he formalized the United States' recognition, and he also made it clear that the executive branch was in agreement with Congress' recognition in late 2019.[^cite_note-557] It was the first time an American President had used the term "Genocide" formally on April 24, the international day of remembrance for the Armenian Genocide.[^cite_note-558][^cite_note-559] On May 6, 2021, the [Latvian Parliament](499765.md) recognized the Armenian genocide.[^cite_note-560] On November 11, 2021, the Regional [Parliament of La Rioja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliament_of_La_Rioja) in [Spain](26667.md) adopted a motion recognizing the Armenian genocide.[^cite_note-561]

On May 6, 2022, [Mississippi](16949861.md) became the 50th US state to recognize the Armenian genocide, with [Governor Tate Reeve's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tate_Reeves) proclamation marking April as "Genocide Awareness and Prevention Month".[^cite_note-562]

On February 8, 2023, the [Mexican Senate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican_Senate) (Senate of the Republic) adopted a document recognizing the Armenian Genocide, citing the need for protection of universal human rights.[^cite_note-563]
